A handle that sits on or next to the origin's line along an axis it scales is left out, as the top and left handles of a sprite whose origin is its top left corner are, and where the move square and a handle overlap, the square is taken. Holding Shift while scaling keeps the aspect ratio. Dragging a scale handle through the origin mirrors the object with a negative scale.
- Each drag is one undoable step, recorded when it ends ("Move hero", "Rotate hero", "Scale hero"); Undo restores the object's fields exactly, an unset `scaleX` or `scaleY` staying unset, and an edge handle leaves the other axis's field alone. A drag that ends where it began records nothing.
- Escape, a gesture the system cancels or a second finger coming down puts the object back as it was before the drag and records nothing; the rest of that gesture does nothing. Undo, Redo, Play, Save, Delete, Duplicate, or changing the tool or the view mode during a drag cancels it first.
- A scale that would be zero or nearly so (below 0.001), or a scale or rotation that would shear a sprite in the object's tree, its ancestors included (a rotated child under a non-uniformly scaled parent, which `ScaleObject` and `RotateObject` refuse too), is not taken: the object keeps its last valid transform and the status bar says why.
- A nested child carries the gizmo too and is dragged in its parent's space, so it follows the pointer under a turned or scaled parent. An object hidden or locked in the scene tree, or under one that is, has no gizmo and is not dragged, even while it is selected.

Drags map through the object's layer: a world-space layer's objects move by the world distance the pointer travels, whatever the 2D view's or the scene camera's zoom and rotation, and a screen-space layer's by the distance on the virtual screen. A tap still selects, and with the Move tool still puts the selected object where it is pressed. A drag with Space held, or a middle-button drag, pans the view instead.

In Go, `BeginDrag2D(x, y)` starts a drag at a canvas point and returns the handle it took (`GizmoHandle2D`), `UpdateDrag2D(x, y, keepAspect)` follows the pointer, and `EndDrag2D` or `CancelDrag2D` finishes it; `PickGizmo2D` names the handle under a canvas point, `Dragging2D` the one held, and `Gizmo2DShown` says whether the gizmo is drawn.

### 2D component shape handles (#882)

In 2D edit mode, the viewport draws every registered shape field (`FieldPoint`, `FieldRect`, `FieldCircle` and `FieldPolygon`) of the selected object in its local space (accounting for parent transforms, rotation, scale, layer space and parallax). Toggling **View > Show All Component Shapes** (or the Command Palette, `SetShowAllShapes`, `CmdToggleAllShapes`, `Editor.ShowAllShapes`) draws shape fields of all visible objects in the scene at once, keeping the selected object highlighted on top.

With the Select or Move tool, interactive handles appear on the selected object's shape fields:

- **Point**: drag the handle to move `(x, y)`.
- **Rectangle**: drag any of the 4 corner or 4 edge handles to resize (dragging past the opposite edge flips smoothly while keeping `width >= 0` and `height >= 0`), or drag the center handle when it does not overlap the object's origin move square.
- **Circle**: drag any of the 4 cardinal rim handles to change `radius` (clamped `>= 0`), or drag the center handle when offset from the object's origin.
- **Polygon**: drag any vertex handle to move it; click or drag an edge midpoint handle to insert a new vertex (`AddPolygonVertex`); right-click or Alt+click a vertex handle to remove it (`RemovePolygonVertex`, keeping at least 3 vertices).
- **Grid snapping**: toggle **Snap** in the 2D viewport bar (`viewport-bar-snap`, **View > Snap to Grid**, `SetSnapToGrid`, `CmdToggleSnapGrid`, `Editor.SnapToGrid`) or configure snapping in the Snap Settings popover (`view-snap-settings`, **View > Snap Settings...**, `CmdSnapSettings`) to snap handle edits and inserted vertices to `EffectiveGridStep` (or whole points when pixel snapping is enabled; holding `Ctrl` / `Cmd` inverts snapping during a drag).

Every drag, vertex addition, and vertex removal commits through `SetComponentField` as one undoable step, and Escape, a cancelled gesture, a second finger, Undo/Redo, Play, Save or switching tools cancels an in-progress drag and restores the pre-drag field unchanged. In Go, `VisibleShapes2D`, `ShapeHandles2D`, `PickShapeHandle2D`, `BeginShapeDrag2D`, `UpdateShapeDrag2D`, `EndShapeDrag2D`, `CancelShapeDrag2D`, `DraggingShape2D`, `ActiveShapeHandle2D`, `AddPolygonVertex`, `RemovePolygonVertex` and `HandleCanvasSecondaryPress` expose the same workflow, and the inspector displays editable controls for every shape field kind.

### Snapping and 2D grid overlay (#869)

The editor provides unified snapping across object placement, dragging, 2D handles (transform gizmo, component shapes, 2D lights/occluders, and 2D navigation regions), and 3D gizmos (translate, rotate, and scale):

- **Snap settings in the viewport bar and menus**: toggle grid snapping with the 2D viewport bar's **Snap** switch (`viewport-bar-snap`), **View > Snap to Grid**, or `CmdToggleSnapGrid` (`SetSnapToGrid`), or open the compact Snap Settings popover (`view-snap-settings` in the 2D viewport bar, `view3d-snap-settings` in the 3D bottom bar, **View > Snap Settings...**, or `CmdSnapSettings`) to switch each mode independently:
  - **Grid snap** (`SnapToGrid`, `SetSnapToGrid`): snaps positions to `EffectiveGridStep()`, which defaults to the active layer's tilemap tile size (supporting non-square tiles) when `GridSize` is `0`, falling back to `8` when the active layer has no tilemap. Setting an explicit step (`SetGridSize`) overrides the tile size until **Auto** (`ResetGridSize`) is pressed.
  - **Pixel snap** (`SnapToPixel`, `SetSnapToPixel`, `CmdToggleSnapPixel`): snaps positions to whole points (1-unit increments) when grid snap is off.
  - **Rotation snap** (`SnapRotation`, `SetSnapRotation`, `CmdToggleSnapRotation`): snaps 2D and 3D rotation drags to multiples of `RotationSnapDeg` degrees in radians (`SetRotationSnapDeg`, default `15°`).
  - **Scale snap** (`SnapScale`, `SetSnapScale`, `CmdToggleSnapScale`): snaps 2D and 3D scale drags to multiples of `ScaleSnapStep` (`SetScaleSnapStep`, default `0.25`) without collapsing scales to zero.
  - **Modifier inversion**: holding `Ctrl` or `Cmd` during a drag or placement press temporarily inverts snapping—disabling active snapping for free precision edits, or enabling snapping when it is off (`SetSnapInvert`).
- **Optional 2D grid overlay** (`ShowGrid`, `SetShowGrid`, `CmdToggleShowGrid`): draws a hairline grid across the visible 2D canvas at the active snap step even outside tile tools, following `View2D` pan, zoom, rotation, and active-layer space/parallax, doubling drawn spacing automatically when zoomed far out so the grid never obscures the canvas.
- **Multi-object selection moves as a whole**: select multiple objects with `SelectObjects`, `ToggleSelectObject`, or Shift-click in 2D `ToolSelect` (`SelectedIDs`). Dragging any selected object (or using the Move tool or 3D Translate gizmo) snaps the primary anchor and moves every other non-descendant selected object by the exact same snapped delta, preserving relative offsets and recording one undoable step (Escape cancels and restores all objects).
- **Per-project preferences**: snap settings (`SnapSettings`, `SetSnapSettings`) are editor preferences saved per project in `.game/editor.yaml` (`ViewStateFile`), never written to scene files and never marking a scene dirty. Switching scenes within the same project keeps the project's snap settings; switching projects saves and restores each project's own preferences.

## 3D viewport (#890)

In 3D the viewport shows the scene as the game draws it: a `gpu.View` fills the panel and draws the scene's 3D build with render3d, and the canvas over it draws the ground grid, the origin axes, the selection outline and the gizmo. The build is `scenemodel.Build`'s (`Scene3D`): every object's built-in primitive (`mesh`) or glTF model (`model`, `modelNode`, `modelMesh`) with its materials and textures, the objects that instanced scenes add included. Models, material files and images load through the editor's `Resolver` when it implements `scenemodel.ModelResolver`, `sceneformat.MaterialResolver` or `scenemodel.ImageResolver`, and from the open project's files otherwise. When the scene does not build, such as when a model it names does not load, a line in the viewport's corner says why (`Viewport3DError`) and every object keeps a box.

- **Lights and sky**: when a version 4 scene defines lights or an `environment`, the viewport renders the scene's own lights, shadows, sky (solid color, gradient, or HDR cubemap via `scenemodel.HDRResolver`), ambient/IBL, fog, tonemapping, and exposure through `scenemodel.Renderer` (`PrepareScene`, `RecordShadows`, `DrawSky`, `ClearColor`), matching the game's render path pixel for pixel. When a scene defines neither lights nor an environment, the viewport falls back to `DefaultLights3D` under a gradient sky. An editor built on this package can still override lighting with `SetLights3D` (`Editor.Lights3D`), which draws over `backgroundColor`.
- **Shading**: the 3D viewport bar's Shading menu (`view3d-shading`), `View > 3D Shading`, and the commands `view.shading.lit`, `view.shading.unlit` and `view.shading.wireframe` switch `Shading3D` (`SetShading3D`): Lit, Unlit (each surface's textured base color plus its emissive color, without lights), and Wireframe, which lights the geometry and draws the edges of its triangles over it. Shading is a view setting: it is not undoable and is not saved.
- **Picking and nested children (#894)**: every nested object up to 64 levels deep—including children added by instanced and inherited scenes—draws at its composed world transform (`ObjectLocalTransform3D` composed up the parent chain, matching `BuildNodes3D` identically) across meshes, wireframes, light/camera/probe icons, physics debug wireframes, occluders, and component gizmos. A press selects the deepest hit object under the pointer: an object that draws a mesh or model is hit on its triangles through `scene3d` picking, while an object without geometry is hit on its world-space box only where no geometry is hit (with a hit descendant winning over its enclosing ancestor). Pressing a second time on an already-selected nested child steps selection up to its parent—and through to the owning scene instance root—before cycling back to the deepest child; holding `Alt`/`Option` while clicking (`Select3DAtWithModifiers`, `HandleViewport3DPressWithModifiers`, `HandleCanvasPressWithModifiers`) directly selects the owning scene instance root for an instanced child or the immediate parent for a locally nested child (and composes with `Shift` to add and `Mod` to toggle). Hidden and locked objects (with their descendants) are passed over.
- **Parent-space gizmo editing and instance overrides (#894)**: when a nested child is selected, the 3D transform gizmo (`Translate`, `Rotate`, `Scale`, single- or multi-selection) draws at the child's composed world position and orientation; dragging moves or turns the child under the pointer in world space while inverting the parent chain to write the child's local transform in parent space. Dragging an object that comes from an instanced scene or base scene writes an override entry under the owning instance's `overrides` (or `SceneDoc.Overrides` for inherited scenes) without touching the source scene file, round-trips through YAML, and commits as one undoable step.
- **Live edits**: the build is made again only when what the scene looks like changes (an object's look, its place in the tree, an instanced scene or an override, or the project's files). A gizmo drag, an undo of a move, the open animation clip's pose at the playhead and a playing game's changes move the built nodes in place, so a drag rebuilds and uploads no mesh. `ObjectCorners3D` and `ObjectAABB3D` give an object's box as the bounds of its geometry when it has any, and Frame Selection (`f`) frames those bounds, so a model a meter across fills the view.
- **Without a GPU**: on a target where the GPU view is not supported, before its first frame and after a device loss, the canvas draws the whole wireframe view instead: the scene's background, the grid, a box around every object (its geometry's bounds when it has any) and the gizmo. `Viewport3DOnGPU` reports which one shows.

`OrbitCamera3D.SceneCamera` turns the editor's orbit camera into the `scene3d.Camera` the GPU view draws through, with the same eye, projection and clip planes the canvas projects with, so the overlays line up with the geometry.

### Navigating the 3D view (#891)

Navigating moves only the editor's camera (`Camera3D`, an `OrbitCamera3D`): it is never an edit, never goes into the undo history and never changes the scene file. The camera jumps where it is told without animating.

| Do | With a mouse | With a touchpad or touch screen | With the keyboard or a button |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Orbit about the target | Drag | Drag with one finger | The Orbit 15° left and right buttons under the axis widget |
| Pan | Middle-button drag, or Shift+drag | Two-finger scroll, or a two-finger drag on a touch screen | |
| Zoom toward the target | Wheel | Pinch, or scroll with Control or Command held | |
| Fly | Hold the right button: move the pointer to look, W, A, S, D to move, Q and E down and up, Shift to go faster, the wheel to change the speed | | |
| Focus the selection | | | `f` or the bottom bar's Focus `F` button, framing its bounds, or the whole scene with nothing selected |
| Axis views | | Click an axis button (`X`, `Y`, `Z` or negative ends) on the orientation widget in the top right corner | `1` Front, `ctrl+1` Back, `3` Right, `ctrl+3` Left, `7` Top, `ctrl+7` Bottom, or Tab/Enter on the orientation widget |
| Perspective or orthographic | | | `5`, or Persp and Ortho in the top-left viewport bar |
| Shading mode | | | The top-left viewport bar's Shading menu (`Lit`, `Unlit`, `Wireframe`) |
| Transform space (Local / Global) | | | `t`, Local / Global in the bottom bar, or command `gizmo.3d.space` |
| Back to the default view | | | The top-left viewport bar's Reset view button |

- **Mouse navigation mappings (`Editor.Navigation3D`, `Navigation3D.Resolve`, Settings › Viewport › 3D navigation)**: choose between three desktop mouse presets (touchpad scrolling, pinch zoom, and two-finger touch panning remain unchanged across all three):
  - **Default (`Navigation3DDefault`)**: left-drag on empty space orbits (and drags gizmos/tools when started on them), Shift+left-drag or middle-drag pans, right-drag flies, and the mouse wheel dollies.
  - **Blender (`Navigation3DBlender`)**: middle-drag orbits, Shift+middle-drag pans, right-drag flies, the mouse wheel dollies, and left-drag is reserved for selection, tools, and gizmos.
  - **Maya (`Navigation3DMaya`)**: Alt+left-drag orbits, middle-drag (or Alt+middle-drag) pans, Alt+right-drag or the mouse wheel dollies, plain right-drag flies, and plain left-drag is reserved for selection, tools, and gizmos.
- **The viewport HUD and status bar** follow the editor's design spec tokens: the top-left bar pairs Persp/Ortho, the Shading menu (`Lit`, `Unlit`, `Wireframe`), the current view name (`ViewName3D`: an axis view name or `Perspective`, with ` · ortho` in orthographic and `User · ortho` when orbited off-axis), Reset view, and Camera/Env toggles, with the keycap help line directly underneath (updating live with the active 3D navigation mapping, e.g. `Drag orbit · Middle pan · Right WASD fly · F focus` in Default mode). The top-right circular orientation widget exposes clickable, keyboard-focusable buttons for all six axis ends above the Orbit 15° left and right buttons. The bottom bar holds the `Local` / `Global` transform space toggle (`GizmoSpace3D`, `SetGizmoSpace3D`), Snap settings popover (`view3d-snap-settings`), 3D Overlays popover (`view3d-overlays`: LOD, Occluders, Render Stats, 3D NavMesh), GridMap tools (`Paint`, `Erase`, `Pick`, `Box`), and `Focus F` button. The status bar adds the view's yaw and pitch in degrees (` · yaw N° · pitch M°`) and the active gizmo space (` · Local` or ` · Global`).
- **Orthographic** (`SetOrthographic3D`, `OrbitCamera3D.Orthographic`) projects in parallel. The view is `OrthoHeight` high, the height the perspective view shows at the target, so switching keeps what lies there the same size, and zooming changes that height. `Project`, `ScreenRay`, `SceneCamera`, picking and the gizmo work the same in both: in orthographic every pixel's ray runs along the view, so a press picks what lies under it and a gizmo drag moves the object by the distance the pointer travels along the axis. An axis view in orthographic is a true plan or elevation.
- **Flying** (`BeginFly3D`, `FlyLook3D`, `FlyMove3D`, `EndFly3D`) turns the camera about its eye and moves eye and target together. While it flies, W, A, S, D, Q and E, with or without Shift, move the camera instead of running their usual shortcuts (the gizmo's W and E, Rotate's and Erase Tile's E), and the pointer is locked to the viewport where the platform can lock it. A flight looks in perspective, so an orthographic view switches to perspective; a right-click that neither turns nor moves the camera leaves it orthographic. Its speed, `FlySpeed3D` in world units a second, starts at the camera's distance from its target and the wheel doubles or halves it (`AdjustFlySpeed3D`).
- **Axis views** (`SetView3D`, the commands `view.3d.front`, `view.3d.back`, `view.3d.right`, `view.3d.left`, `view.3d.top` and `view.3d.bottom`, and `view.3d.orthographic` for the projection) keep the target and the distance. Front looks along -Z, Right along -X and Top straight down with -Z at the top of the view.
- **Saved per scene**: each scene of a project opens at the camera it was left at. The editor writes the views to `.game/editor.yaml` in the project (`ViewStateFile`, described on [File Formats](formats.md)) as another scene opens, as the scene is saved and as the window closes, and an app that ends the editor another way calls `SaveViewState`. A scene it has no view of opens at `DefaultOrbitCamera3D`. Creating the `.game` folder, the editor writes a `.gitignore` there that ignores the folder, so the views stay out of version control. A file a newer editor wrote, with a higher `version`, is neither read nor written over.

### 3D transform gizmos (#892)

The 3D viewport's transform gizmo reuses `render3d.Gizmo` for drawing, picking, and constrained drags in **Global** (world) or **Local** space:

- **Modes and shortcuts**: press `w` (Translate, `Gizmo3DTranslate`), `e` (Rotate, `Gizmo3DRotate`), and `r` (Scale, `Gizmo3DScale`) in the 3D viewport (`ScopeViewport3D`, without clashing with 2D tile tools' `e`), or choose them in the header bar's tool group, `View > 3D Gizmo`, or the Command Palette.
- **Global / Local space toggle**: toggle `Editor.GizmoSpace3D` (`GizmoSpaceGlobal` / `GizmoSpaceLocal`) via the 3D bottom bar's **Local** / **Global** toggle (`view3d-space-local` / `view3d-space-global`), shortcut `t` (`CmdGizmoSpace3D`), `View > 3D Gizmo > 3D Gizmo: Local / Global Space`, or `SetGizmoSpace3D` / `ToggleGizmoSpace3D`. In Local mode, handles orient to the selected object's Gram-Schmidt orthonormalized world orientation—including its ancestors for nested children (`GizmoOrientation3D`).
- **Exact rotation about the drawn axis**: dragging any rotation ring (X, Y, or Z) composes the drag around the exact world-space axis the drawn ring represents and converts the child-local result (`RParentᵀ · RDeltaWorld · RWorldStart`) back to scene Euler XYZ (`Rz·Ry·Rx`), so even on an object already rotated about multiple axes or nested under a rotated or scaled parent, every ring turns strictly about the axis drawn.
- **Plane handles, screen-space center handle, and uniform scale**: Translate exposes `Axis3DXY`, `Axis3DXZ`, and `Axis3DYZ` planar squares (moving strictly within the active two-axis plane, with grazing-ray guards) and a center `Axis3DAll` handle (translating in the camera view plane). Scale exposes a center `Axis3DAll` cube that scales all three axes proportionally.
- **Snapping and live drag delta**: 3D translate, rotate, and scale drags share the editor's snap settings (`SnapSettings`, `EffectiveGridSize`, `EffectiveRotationSnapDeg`, `EffectiveScaleSnapStep`) and temporary `Ctrl`/`Cmd` modifier inversion (`SetSnapInvert`). While dragging, the status bar displays the live delta (`GizmoDragDelta3D`: `dx`/`dy`/`dz`, degrees `°`, or scale factor `x`) alongside the active space (`Global`/`Local`), and every completed drag commits as **one undo step** that round-trips cleanly through scene YAML (`Escape` cancels and restores the pre-drag transform).

### Placing models in 3D (#893)

The asset browser lists, under its atlases, the models to place in 3D (`Models`): the built-in primitives, then the open project's glTF models by path, the outputs of `game assets` model targets included before they are built (`ModelEntry.Output`). Each has a thumbnail rendered offscreen (`ModelThumbnail`): the model alone, framed from above and in front and lit by `DefaultLights3D`, drawn through scenemodel on NSGo's software GPU. Each model loads on the UI goroutine the first time its thumbnail is drawn, as everything the editor's `Resolver` serves loads, and apart from the scene's own cache of models; the rendering runs off the UI goroutine, so the list shows at once and the pictures follow. A model that does not load gets none.

- **Place tool**: pressing a model selects it (`SelectModel`, `SelectedModel`) and, in 3D, turns on the Place tool (also reachable via `p`, `View > Tools > Place`, or `CmdToolPlace`). In 3D a press then adds an object drawing the model, named after it, where the pointer's ray hits the scene's geometry, or where it crosses the ground plane, the horizontal plane at `PlaceHeight3D`, when it hits none (`PlacementAt3D`, `PlaceModelAt3D`). The object rests on that point: it is raised along the surface's normal by how far its geometry reaches below its origin, so a unit box on the floor sits on it rather than half under it. Hidden and locked objects are passed over, as picking passes over them.
- **Snap and Align**: while the Place tool is active in 3D, the contextual Place bar (`view3d-place-bar`)—alongside `View > Tools` and the Command Palette (`CmdTogglePlaceSnap3D`, `CmdTogglePlaceAlign3D`)—exposes **Snap** (`place3d-snap`, snapping the X and Z of a point on the ground plane to the spacing of the grid the viewport draws, `PlaceSnap3D`; a point on the scene's geometry is not snapped, since the grid's spacing can be wider than the object under the pointer), **Align** (`place3d-align`, turning a model placed on the scene's geometry so that its +Y axis follows the surface's normal, `PlaceAlign3D`), and the ground height field (`place3d-height`, setting `PlaceHeight3D`). They are view settings, not edits (`SetPlaceOptions3D`).
- **Drag and drop**: dragging a model from the asset browser onto the 3D viewport places it where it is dropped, through the Place tool's rules, whatever the active tool. A `.gltf` or `.glb` file dropped on the viewport from another app is placed when it lies inside the project's folder, outside the folders the asset browser leaves out (names starting with `.` or `_`, and `testdata`) (`DropModelFiles`), several files as one step; a file outside the project, of another kind or from the web, which names no path, is refused with the reason in the status bar. A file drop carries no position, so dropped files land where the view's middle points.
- **Bottom MODELS strip**: in 3D view (outside Assets drawer mode), a **MODELS** strip (`Viewport3DModelsStripView`, `StripModels`) sits along the bottom of the 3D viewport (`"drag in"`), showing offscreen-rendered thumbnails of the project's models first followed by built-in primitives, each with the tooltip `"Drag into the viewport to place <name>"`, click-to-select and drag-into-viewport placement, and a pinned **Or ask for one** button (`OpenModelAgentPrompt`) that opens an inline agent prompt (`"Describe a model… e.g. a low stone planter, 1 × 0.5 m, same stone as the walls"`) dispatching `Viewport3DAgentRequest` to `Editor.OnAskAgent3D` (`SubmitViewport3DAgentPrompt`).
- **One step**: every placement is one undoable step, which Undo takes back and Redo makes again. It is refused while playing, on a locked active layer and on a screen-space one, and for a model that does not load; placing a model in a version 1 scene raises it to the current version, as `PlaceObject` does.

### 3D lights, environment and scene cameras (#895)

Version 4 scenes carry their own 3D lighting, environment, and 3D camera objects, edited directly in the 3D viewport and panels:

- **Icons and picking**: visible directional, point, and spot lights and 3D cameras draw crisp, color-tinted icons in the 3D viewport overlay instead of generic marker cubes; clicking an icon selects its object (`Pick3D`).
- **Interactive undoable handles**: selecting a light or 3D camera reveals wireframe guides and draggable handles (`Handle3DKind`, `HandlePosition3D`, `PickHandle3D`, `BeginHandleDrag3D`, `UpdateHandleDrag3D`, `EndHandleDrag3D`, `CancelHandleDrag3D`):
  - Directional light: direction arrow and aim handle (`Handle3DLightDirection`).
  - Point light: three orthogonal range circles and range radius handle (`Handle3DLightRange`).
  - Spot light: aim handle (`Handle3DLightDirection`), range distance handle (`Handle3DLightRange`), and separate outer (`Handle3DLightOuterCone`) and inner (`Handle3DLightInnerCone`) cone rim handles.
  - 3D camera: frustum wireframe with vertical FOV / orthographic height handle (`Handle3DCameraFOV`) and far plane handle (`Handle3DCameraFar`).
  Every drag updates the live GPU viewport in place without rebuilding meshes and commits as one undoable step on `Editor.History`.
- **Scene Environment panel (`EnvironmentPanelView`)**: toggled from the 3D viewport bar's **Env** button, `View > Scene Environment`, or `CmdEnvironmentPanel` (`EnvironmentPanelOpen` / `SetEnvironmentPanelOpen`), editing sky (`none`, `color`, `gradient`, `hdr`), ambient color and intensity, IBL intensity, distance and height fog, tonemapping (`none`, `reinhard`, `aces`, `neutral`, `agx`), and exposure through `SetEnvironment` (validated and undoable, upgrading scenes below version 4 to version 4 and restoring the prior version on Undo).
- **Inspector and creation helpers**: in 3D view (or on any object with a light or 3D camera), the inspector exposes `LIGHT 3D` (`SetObjectLight`) and `CAMERA 3D` (`SetObjectCamera3D`, switching `current` atomically across cameras in one undo step). In Go, `AddLight3D(kind)` and `AddCamera3D(proj)` place pre-configured lights and 3D cameras on the active world layer, and `ProjectHDRs()` lists `.hdr`/`.pic` skies in the open project.
- **Look Through Scene Camera**: press `0`, click **Camera** in the 3D viewport bar, or run `CmdView3DCamera` (`SetLookThroughCamera3D` / `ToggleLookThroughCamera3D`) to view the 3D viewport through the scene's active 3D camera (`ActiveSceneCamera3D`), matching the game's first frame pixel for pixel. Orbiting, panning, zooming, flying, or choosing an axis view exits camera preview cleanly.

### Reflection probes and light probe grids (#903)

Version 4 scenes also support local 3D reflection probes (`ObjectDoc.ReflectionProbe`) and 3D light probe grids (`ObjectDoc.LightProbeGrid`) baked directly from the editor so runtime shading in `scenemodel` matches the editor 3D viewport pixel for pixel:

- **Placement & inspector**: place probes with `AddReflectionProbe3D` / `AddLightProbeGrid3D` (or command palette `CmdAddReflectionProbe` / `CmdAddLightProbeGrid`) and edit their local box bounds (`center`, `size`), blend distance, intensity, priority, box projection, cubemap resolution, grid counts, and baked file paths in the inspector (`REFLECTION PROBE` via `SetObjectReflectionProbe`, `LIGHT PROBE GRID` via `SetObjectLightProbeGrid`) as undoable steps.
- **Viewport icons, wireframes, and 3D bounds handles**: reflection probes draw a cyan chrome-sphere icon, outer influence wireframe box, and inner blend wireframe box; light probe grids draw an amber dot-grid icon, bounds wireframe box, and projected sample points. Selecting either exposes six draggable face-center bounds handles (`Handle3DProbeBoundsPosX`..`Handle3DProbeBoundsNegZ`) that resize the box while anchoring the opposite face, commit as a single undoable step (`EndHandleDrag3D`), and cancel cleanly with Escape (`CancelHandleDrag3D`).
- **Baking**: click **Bake Cubemap** / **Bake Probes** in the inspector, **Bake Probes** in the Scene Environment panel, or run `CmdBakeProbes3D` (`BakeReflectionProbe3D`, `BakeLightProbeGrid3D`, `BakeAllProbes3D`). Reflection probes render the 3D scene from the probe origin and write a lossless 6×1 Radiance `.hdr` cubemap strip (`render3d.EncodeCubemapHDR`); light probe grids sample the scene at every grid point and write L2 spherical harmonic irradiance coefficients as deterministic versioned YAML (`*.probes.yaml`, `render3d.EncodeLightProbeGrid`, schema `https://game.nsgo.dev/schemas/lightprobes.v1.json`). Output paths are validated against traversal/symlinks/reserved filenames, written atomically, linked onto the probe undoably, and loaded by `scenemodel.Build` and `scenemodel.Renderer` so game and editor shading match identically.

## Tile palette (#874)

While the Paint, Erase, Fill, Pick or Terrain tool is active in 2D, the tile palette shows right of the viewport. It shows the tiles of the active layer's tileset as a grid in tile ID order, each at the tilemap's tile size (a tile wider than the panel is scaled down to fit it). The tiles are the tileset atlas's frames whose names are tile IDs (`"1"`, `"2"`, …), as the scene format looks tiles up; other frames are left out. Pressing a tile, or moving between tiles with the arrow keys, makes it the tile Paint and Fill write (`SetActiveTileID`); every arrow key steps through the tiles in ID order, Left and Up to the previous tile and Right and Down to the next, not by rows of the grid. The selected tile is ringed, and with Paint or Fill the status bar shows its ID. Choosing a tile is not an edit and does not go into the undo history; the paint that follows is one undoable step.

The palette follows the active layer: when it changes, the palette shows the new layer's tileset, and if that tileset has no tile with the selected ID, its first tile is selected. A layer with no inline tilemap (none, or an external Tiled or LDtk map), a tileset the resolver cannot resolve and a tileset without tile frames each show a message saying so instead of an empty grid. In Go, `TilePalette()` returns what the palette shows for the active layer: its tileset, tile size and tiles, or the message.

For an inline or layered grid naming a `tilesetFile`, the palette shows the tiles of its YAML tileset, read through the resolver's `sceneformat.FileResolver` (such as `FSAssets`), with IDs from 1 as `PaintTile` takes them: tile ID `n` paints the tileset's local tile `n - 1`. The palette's labels and the status bar name each tile by its local ID, the number the rows and the tileset's `tiles[].id` write (`PaletteTile.Number`), and a footer note reminds you that collision, terrain and variant rules live in the tileset file. Above the tiles, a toggle per tile layer (for layered grids) picks the one Paint, Erase, Fill and Terrain change (`SetActiveTileLayer`, `ActiveTileLayer`, the first by default), the H, V and D toggles set the flips Paint and Fill place tiles with (`SetActiveTileFlip`, `ActiveTileFlip`), the terrain toggles pick the terrain set Terrain paints (`SetActiveTerrain`, `ActiveTerrain`), and **Edit Tileset** opens the layer's `*.tileset.yaml` in the Tileset Editor. While a 2D tile tool is active on a YAML tileset layer with no object selected, the right-side **Tile Inspector** shows the picked tile's swatch, display name and source cell, a `COLLISION` toggle (`Solid`, `One-way`, `None`), its terrain/collision/variant-weight rules, and a button to open the full Tileset Editor. The edits write the layer's rows in the form `Encode` saves, one undoable step each, and Fill joins cells whose tile and flips both match.

## Painting, erasing and picking tiles (#875)

With Paint or Erase, a drag with the mouse or one finger paints or erases every cell it crosses, however fast it moves, and the whole stroke is one undoable step, so one Undo takes back a row painted in one drag. A click or tap paints or erases its one cell. With Paint, a right-button drag erases. A stroke that starts off the map paints once it reaches it, a stroke that changes nothing records nothing, and a drag with Space held pans the view instead. A second finger cancels a one-finger stroke and puts its cells back, so a two-finger pan never paints.

While a tile tool is active, the cell under the mouse is outlined, snapped to the grid: with Paint and Fill it shows the selected tile faded in the cell, with Terrain it shows the autotiled tile faded in emerald, with Erase it is tinted red, and when a press would pick it is outlined in amber.

Picking takes the tile in a cell as the tile to paint, and the palette selects it: Alt+click (or Alt+drag) with any tile tool, or the **Pick** tool (`ToolPickTile`, reachable from the tile palette's **Pick** button, `View > Tools > Pick Tile`, or `CmdToolPickTile`), which then hands over to Paint. An empty cell leaves the selected tile as it was. Picking is not an edit and does not go into the undo history.

In Go, `BeginTileStroke(mode, x, y)`, `ContinueTileStroke` and `EndTileStroke` draw a stroke (`TileStrokePaint`, `TileStrokeErase`, `TileStrokePick`, `TileStrokeTerrain` or `TileStrokeEraseTerrain`) at canvas points, `CancelTileStroke` puts it back and `TileStrokeActive` reports one under way; painting and erasing refuse locked or hidden layers, and choosing a tool or layer, changing layers, switching views, saving, Undo, Redo and Play end a stroke under way first (while Revert cancels it). `PickTile(layer, col, row)` picks, `SetTileHover` and `ClearTileHover` move the outline and `HoveredTile` returns the cell it is on.

## Terrain painting and tileset editor (#878, #879)

Inline and layered tilemaps backed by `*.tileset.yaml` share their tile flags, collision shapes, animations, custom data and autotiled terrain sets between the runtime (`tilemap.ApplyTerrain`, `tilemap.SetTerrainTile`, `physics.MoveAndSlide`, `tileshape.New`) and the editor:

- **Terrain brush (`ToolTerrain`, shortcut `t`)**: drags place terrain (`TileStrokeTerrain`) and right-button drags erase terrain (`TileStrokeEraseTerrain`), updating each touched cell and its surrounding neighbors with the exact same 4-bit cardinal (`cardinal`) and 8-bit corner-gated (`blob`) rules as `tilemap.Tilemap.SetAutotile`. The entire stroke — both placed/erased cells and every updated neighbor — is recorded as one undoable step, so one Undo restores the stroke cleanly. In Go, `PaintTerrain(layer, col, row, terrain)` and `EraseTerrain(layer, col, row, terrain)` perform single-cell edits.
- **Tileset Editor (`TilesetEditorPanelView`)**: open any `*.tileset.yaml` from the tile palette's **Edit Tileset** button (`OpenActiveLayerTileset`), the Tile Inspector, double-clicking or pressing **Open** / **Edit Tileset** in the file browser (`OpenTilesetProjectFile`), or `OpenTilesetFile` / `OpenTilesetYAML`. The left atlas grid previews every tile with badges for solid/one-way collision, custom shapes, animation, terrain masks and default tiles, while four tabs edit each tile:
  - **Terrain**: create or delete 4-bit (`cardinal`) and 8-bit (`blob`) terrain sets (`AddTilesetTerrain`, `RemoveTilesetTerrain`, `SetTilesetTerrainMode`), set an explicit fallback default tile (`SetTilesetTerrainDefault`), and assign each tile's neighbor bitmask by clicking its center, edges (`N`, `E`, `S`, `W`) and — in 8-bit `blob` mode — corners (`NW`, `NE`, `SW`, `SE`) (`ToggleTilesetEdgeOrCorner`, `ToggleTilesetTileCenter`, `SetTilesetTileMask`). Corner clicks enforce corner-gating automatically (enabling a corner enables both adjacent edges; clearing an edge clears dependent corners). If an intermediate click matches a mask owned by another tile, the editor leaves that tile intact so you can keep clicking or press **Reassign** (`ReassignTilesetEditMask`).
  - **Collision**: toggle `Solid`, `One-way`, `None`, or custom flag bits (`SetTilesetTileCollision`, `SetTilesetTileFlags`, `ToggleTilesetTileFlagBit`), and draw rectangular (`rect`) or multi-vertex (`polygon`) collision shapes directly over the magnified tile canvas (`AddTilesetTileShape`, `UpdateTilesetTileShape`, `RemoveTilesetTileShape`, `AddTilesetDraftPolygonPoint`, `CommitTilesetDraftPolygon`).
  - **Animation**: build and reorder per-tile animation frames with durations (`AddTilesetTileAnimFrame`, `UpdateTilesetTileAnimFrame`, `RemoveTilesetTileAnimFrame`, `SetTilesetTileAnimation`) alongside a live playing/pausable frame preview (`SetTilesetAnimPlaying`, `TilesetAnimPreviewTile`). Animated tiles advance live in the 2D editor viewport as well as in Play mode and the game (`StepTileAnimations`).
  - **Data**: edit each tile's display `name`, variant `weight`, and typed custom properties (`bool`, `int`, `float`, `string`) (`SetTilesetTileName`, `SetTilesetTileWeight`, `SetTilesetTileProperty`, `DeleteTilesetTileProperty`).
  - Every tileset edit goes through the editor's `History` (undoable/redoable), takes effect immediately in the viewport, Play mode and Terrain brush before saving, and saves back as canonical YAML (`SaveTileset`, `SaveTilesetYAML`). Agents can read and edit the same fields atomically via the editor MCP tools `tileset_read` and `tileset_edit` ([MCP](mcp.md#tools)).

## 3D GridMap painting (#899)

In 3D view (`ViewMode3D`), the editor opens and paints modular 3D [`*.gridmap.yaml`](world3d.md#modular-3d-gridmap) files directly on an editable floor grid:

- **Opening and attaching**: set an object's `gridMap` field in the inspector (`SetObjectGridMap`) or open a `*.gridmap.yaml` file (`OpenGridMap`, `OpenGridMapFS`, `OpenGridMapDoc`, `NewGridMap`). Switching to a GridMap tool in the 3D bottom bar (**Paint**, **Erase**, **Pick**, or **Box**), **View > Tools**, or the Command Palette (`CmdGridMapPaint`, `CmdGridMapErase`, `CmdGridMapPick`, `CmdGridMapBoxFill`) automatically opens the selected object's `gridMap` when none is open yet, and `MoveFile` tracks the open GridMap's path and `meshLibrary` reference.
- **Palette with 3D thumbnails**: while a GridMap tool is active in 3D view, `GridMapPaletteView` (`GridMapPalette()`) appears right of the viewport, rendering an isometric shaded 3D thumbnail for every tile in the glTF `meshLibrary` alongside controls for the active floor height and 24-orientation index.
- **Floor height and 24 orientations**: press `]` / `PageUp` and `[` / `PageDown` (`SetGridMapFloor`, `StepGridMapFloor`) to move the active horizontal painting plane up or down by one cell. Press `X`, `Y`, `Z` (with `Shift` for reverse) or `Q` / `Shift+Q` (`RotateGridMapOrientation`, `StepGridMapOrientation`, `SetGridMapOrientation`) to rotate the active tile through all 24 axis-aligned cube orientations (`world3d.RotateGridOrientation`).
- **Painting, erasing, picking and box fill**: hovering in the 3D viewport projects the pointer ray onto the active floor (`ScreenToGridMapCell`) and draws a live hovered cell preview (and a dashed rectangular preview while dragging **Box** fill). Clicking or dragging paints or erases every cell crossed as a single undoable stroke (`BeginGridMapStroke`, `ContinueGridMapStroke`, `EndGridMapStroke`, `CancelGridMapStroke`, `Escape` cancels), **Pick** (or Alt+click) adopts the tile and orientation under the pointer, and **Box** drags out a filled rectangle on the active floor (`FillGridMapBox`).
- **Undo and canonical save**: every paint stroke, erase stroke, box fill, and cell rotation (`PaintGridMapCell`, `EraseGridMapCell`, `FillGridMapBox`, `RotateGridMapCell`) goes through the editor's shared `History` (Undo/Redo). `Save` (`SaveGridMap`, `SaveGridMapFile`) writes canonical deterministic `.gridmap.yaml` via `world3d.GridMap.ToDoc` and `world3d.EncodeGridMap`, so saved files round-trip byte-for-byte and instance identically in `scenemodel.Build`.

## Rectangle, line, stamp and random tile brushes (#876)

Four additional tile brushes extend inline and layered tilemap painting, each showing a live preview in the 2D viewport before release, cancelling cleanly on **Escape** (or a second touch) without mutating the scene, and committing every changed cell as **one undoable step** in `Editor.History`:

- **Rectangle (`ToolRectTile`, shortcut `u`)**: drag between two cells to fill the rectangle with `ActiveTileID` (`TileStrokeRect`, `FillTileRect`), or right-drag to erase the dragged rectangle (`TileStrokeRectErase`, `EraseTileRect`).
- **Line (`ToolLineTile`, shortcut `l`)**: drag between two cells to draw a Bresenham line of `ActiveTileID` (`TileStrokeLine`, `DrawTileLine`), or right-drag to erase along the line (`TileStrokeLineErase`, `EraseTileLine`).
- **Stamp and region clipboard (`ToolStampTile`, shortcut `c`)**: paint a rectangular `TileStamp` pattern anchored to the click or pattern-aligned across a drag (`TileStrokeStamp`, `StampTiles`), preserving per-cell flip bits on layered tilemaps and empty (`0`) cells from copied regions. Build a stamp either by selecting a rectangle of tiles in the tile palette (`SelectPaletteStamp`, `SelectPaletteStampRange`, or clicking an anchor tile and pressing **Rect** or holding Shift before clicking the opposite corner) or by right-dragging a map region (`TileStrokeSelectRegion`, `SetTileSelection`) and using **Copy** (`mod+c`, `CopyTileRegion`, `CopySelectedTileRegion`), **Cut** (`mod+x`, `CutTileRegion`, `CutSelectedTileRegion`, one undo step) and **Paste** (`mod+v`, `PasteTileRegion`).
- **Random (`ToolRandomTile`, shortcut `n`)**: paint a deterministic weighted pick from multiple tiles (`SetRandomBrushTiles`, `SetRandomBrushTileWeight`, `ToggleRandomBrushTile`) controlled by a fixed seed (`SetRandomBrushSeed`, `RandomBrushSeed`). In the tile palette, clicking tiles toggles them in the weighted pool with per-tile `-`/`+` weight controls and a Seed field; right-drag erases.

In Go, `TilePreview()` inspects the live preview cells of an in-progress brush stroke before `EndTileStroke`, `TileStamp` / `TileClipboard` / `TileSelection` manage patterns and regions, and all four brushes work identically on single-grid and layered YAML tilemaps while keeping deterministic YAML round-trip saves.

## Agent tile tools and region-based tile edit review (#1064)

Coding agents connected to the editor's MCP server ([MCP](mcp.md#tools)) can inspect and paint inline and layered tilemaps while the author reviews changes by connected region rather than individual cells:

- **Query and paint tools**: `tile_query` (`Editor.QueryTiles`) finds matching cells or empty pockets by tile ID, YAML tileset `tileName`, or sub-rectangle (for example, `"an 11 × 5 pocket at columns 2–12, rows 12–16"`). `tile_paint` (`Editor.AgentPaintTiles`) paints explicit cells, inclusive rectangles, or deterministic seeded weighted tile picks (with optional `noAdjacentDuplicates`). `tile_autotile` (`Editor.AgentAutotile`) re-edges or places a YAML tileset terrain set using the same 4-bit `cardinal` / 8-bit `blob` rules as `ToolTerrain`. `tile_erase` (`Editor.AgentEraseTiles`) clears cells or rectangles. Each call applies as one undoable edit and joins the active tile review by default (`review: true`).
- **Region-based review (`TileReviewPanelView`)**: pending agent changes are grouped into connected regions by kind — **painted** (`+`, green dashed outline), **replaced** (`~`, amber dashed outline), and **erased** (`−`, red solid outline) — each showing its cell count, bounding rect, and human-readable detail (such as `"from 4 cave tiles"`). Clicking a region focuses it in the 2D viewport; each region can be independently toggled between **Revert** and **Restore** (`SetTileReviewRegionReverted`, `ToggleTileReviewRegion`). **Before / After** and **hold `B`** (`SetTileReviewViewMode`, `SetTileReviewPeekBefore`, `TileReviewHandleKeyEvent`) preview the pre-review tilemap live. **Keep N of M** (`KeepTileReview`) commits all kept regions cleanly as **one** undoable `History` step (so one Undo restores the exact pre-review tilemap), and **Revert all** (`RevertAllTileReview`) restores the original tilemap immediately. Agents and tests can also drive reviews via `tile_review_status`, `tile_review_revert_region`, `tile_review_restore_region`, `tile_review_keep`, and `tile_review_revert_all`.
- **Palette & viewport caption**: while a tile layer is selected in 2D, the viewport shows a top-left layer dimension caption (`ActiveTileLayerCaption`, e.g., `World · 24 × 20 cells · 16 px`), and the tile palette displays `TILESET <file>` with a selected-tile readout showing the picked tile's swatch, name, atlas cell coordinates, collision rule, and terrain set.

## Scene tree (#861)

The scene tree panel (`SceneTreeView`), at the left of the editor, lists the open scene's layers and, under each, its objects with their children nested, as Godot's Scene dock and Unity's Hierarchy do. It follows every edit and every undo. Pressing an object's row selects it, and selecting an object in either viewport selects its row and opens the rows above it; pressing a layer's row makes it the active layer. Every object edit works on a nested child as on a top-level object: `FindObject` and `SelectObject` find an object by ID anywhere in the tree, the inspector edits the selected child, and its transform is edited in its parent's space, so the Move tool puts its origin under the press and the 3D gizmo moves it along the world axis.

- **Moving in the tree**: drag a row onto another to make it that object's last child, or near the top or bottom edge of the row to put it before or after it; drop it on a layer's row to move it to the top level of that layer. A row's context menu moves it up or down among its siblings, makes it a child of the sibling above, moves it out of its parent, or moves it to another layer. In Go, `ReparentObject(id, parentID, index)`, `MoveObjectToLayer(id, layer, index)`, `ReorderObject(id, index)` and `DropObject(id, targetID, place)` do the same, each as one undoable step; an index below 0 or past the end puts the object last.
- **World transform**: a moved object keeps its world transform. Its local transform is computed again under its new parent in 2D (`x`, `y`, `rotation`, `scaleX`, `scaleY`), and Undo puts every field back exactly; a flipped object keeps its negative scale on the axis it had. When the object or one of its old or new ancestors sets a 3D field, its depth (`posZ`, `scaleZ`) is kept too. When one of them sets a 3D rotation (`rotX`, `rotY`, `rotZ`), the 2D and 3D views no longer agree on where `x` and `y` lead, and only the 3D world transform is kept: the object stays in place in the 3D viewport, and may move in the 2D one. The move is refused when it would put an object under itself or one of its descendants, nest deeper than `MaxObjectDepth`, or leave a transform that cannot be written as a translation, rotation and scale under the new parent, as a sprite under a turned parent stretched along one axis would be. A move between a world and a screen layer keeps the numbers, not where the object appears on screen.
- **Rename**: F2, a double press or the context menu's Rename edits the selected object's name in its row; Return or leaving the field keeps it, and Escape drops it. Change ID edits its ID through `SetObjectID(oldID, newID)`, which rewrites the scene's connections that name the object, and the objects of a scene it instances (`enemy_1/body` becomes `boss/body`), in the same step, as well as the open animation clip's target and the targets of its tracks, and refuses an ID the scene already uses, including one an instanced or inherited scene adds. Another scene file that instances or inherits this one and overrides the object by its old ID is not rewritten; move such references with `game mv` or edit them by hand.
- **Eye and lock**: the eye button of a row hides the object, and with it its descendants, in both viewports, and the lock button stops a press in either viewport from picking it, so the press reaches what lies under it (`SetObjectHidden`, `SetObjectLocked`). Neither viewport moves a hidden or locked object: its 3D gizmo is not shown and the Move tool leaves it where it is. Both belong to the editing session: they are not edits, Undo does not touch them, they are never written to the scene file, Play draws every object, and opening another scene clears them. A hidden or locked object can still be selected from the tree and edited in the inspector.

The 3D viewport draws and picks every object, nested children included: `Objects3D` lists them depth first, each with its parent's ID, and a child's geometry and box are placed by its ancestors' transforms ([3D viewport](#3d-viewport-890)).

### Object context menu (#1039)

Secondary-clicking or Control-clicking an object in the 2D viewport, the 3D viewport, or a Scene Tree row—or pressing `Shift+F10` or the Context Menu key (`contextmenu`) on the active selection—opens a unified `ui.ContextMenu` headered with the object's display name and kind/layer subtitle (for example, `Sprite · layer actors`). Right-dragging in the 3D viewport still flies the camera, right-dragging with 2D tile tools still erases or selects a tile region, and right-clicking a polygon collider vertex still removes that vertex; actions whose feature does not apply to the target object or session are omitted rather than shown disabled.

- **Copy as YAML (`mod+c`) and Paste (`mod+v`)**: `CopyObjectYAML` / `CopySelectedYAML` (`ObjectYAML`) encodes the object as a canonical `sceneformat` YAML document onto the clipboard (`core.Scope.SetClipboard` or `Editor.WriteClipboard`) that `sceneformat.Parse` round-trips back to the exact same object, and shows a floating viewport toast (`Copied <name> as YAML · N lines`). `Paste` / `PasteObjectYAML` pastes from the clipboard onto the active layer with a fresh unique ID in one undo step.
- **Duplicate (`mod+d`) and Group (`mod+g`)**: `DuplicateObjects` clones the selected object(s), and `GroupObjects` / `GroupSelected` wraps them under a new parent object in one undo step while preserving world transforms.
- **Move under**: the submenu (`MoveUnderCandidates`, `MoveObjectUnder`) lists valid candidate parent objects—excluding the object itself, its descendants, and moves that would exceed `MaxObjectDepth`—annotated with current parent, locked state, child count, or layer, and reparents in one undo step so a single Undo puts the object back.
- **Select same prefab**: when the object sets `prefab` or `scene`, `SelectSamePrefab` selects every object in the scene sharing that reference (`SelectedIDs`, `SelectObjects`, `IsObjectSelected`).
- **Hide / Show (`h`), Lock / Unlock, Find usages (`shift+f12`), Rename (`f2`), and Ask the agent (`mod+l`)**: toggle session visibility (`ToggleObjectHidden`) and lock (`ToggleObjectLocked`), find usages in the open project (`FindObjectUsages` / `ShowObjectUsages`, opening the Find Usages review screen for scene/prefab-path targets or Search for plain IDs, omitted without an open project), start inline renaming in the Scene Tree (`RequestRenameObject`), or send an `AgentPromptRequest` draft to `Editor.OnAskAgent` (`AskAgentAboutObject`). Context menus read live custom shortcuts (`EffectiveShortcut`), expose scene-instance and inherited-object override actions (`Open Instanced Scene`, `Make Local`, `Apply Overrides to Source`, `Open Source Scene`, `Revert Overrides`) while guarding local-only structural edits on foreign objects, and retain Scene Tree order, Cut, Paste as Child, Ungroup, Change ID, Move to Layer, and Delete actions.
- **MCP parity (`pkg/editor/editormcp`)**: every context menu action calls an exported `Editor` method that the editor MCP server exposes (`object_copy_yaml`, `object_paste_yaml`, `object_duplicate`, `object_group`, `object_move_under`, `object_select_same_prefab`, `object_visibility`, and `object_find_usages`, alongside `scene_edit` and `selection_get`'s `selectedIds`).

### Multi-object selection and batch editing (#867)

The editor tracks an ordered selection set with `Editor.SelectedID` as the primary object (`SelectedIDs`, `SelectionCount`, `IsSelected`, `SelectedObjects`, `SelectObjectsWithPrimary`, `AddToSelection`, `RemoveFromSelection`, `ToggleSelection`, `ClearSelection`):

- **Viewport selection and marquee**: In 2D and 3D, `Shift`+click adds an object to the selection and `Mod` (`Cmd`/`Ctrl`)+click toggles membership (`HandleCanvasPressWith`, `HandleViewport3DPressWith`). In 2D with Select, dragging on empty canvas draws a marquee box (`BeginMarquee2DWith`, `UpdateMarquee2D`, `EndMarquee2D`, `CancelMarquee2D`, `SelectMarquee2D`) that selects enclosed objects by default, or touching objects when `Alt` is held (`MarqueeEnclose`, `MarqueeTouch`); `Shift` adds and `Mod` toggles marquee matches. `Escape` cancels an active drag or marquee first, and clears selection at rest.
- **Scene tree synchronization**: `SceneTreeView` follows and drives the same multi-selection set with `Shift`+click and `Mod`+click, expanding ancestors of selected objects.
- **Common-center transforms, Delete and Duplicate**: Move, rotate, and scale gizmos in both 2D and 3D act on the whole selection about its combined bounding-box center (filtering out descendants whose ancestor is also selected so children never double-transform), and `DeleteSelected` / `DuplicateSelected` act on the whole selection; each operation records **one undo step** via `History.Group`. `FrameSelection` frames all selected objects together.
- **Shared-field inspector**: When multiple objects are selected, `InspectorView` shows `"N selected"` and only the sections shared by every selected object (Transform, Sprite when all have sprites, shared Properties and shared Components). Differing values display as `"Mixed"` (or indeterminate for checkboxes), and editing any field writes every selected object in **one undo step** (`EditSelected`, `SetSelectedComponentField`, `SetSelectedProperty`, `SetSelectedX`, …) so a single Undo restores each object's own prior value.

### Copy, paste, duplicate and group objects (#868)

The scene tree supports multi-object selection (`SelectObjects`, `SelectedIDs`, `ToggleSelectObject`, `IsObjectSelected`), and object clipboard, duplication, and grouping actions live in the Edit menu, Scene Tree context menu, Command Palette, and keyboard shortcuts:

- **Copy (`mod+c`, `CopyObjects`, `CopySelected`) and Cut (`mod+x`, `CutObjects`, `CutSelected`)**: serialize the selected object(s) — including nested children, components, properties, `overrides`, and every connection whose endpoints both belong to the copied subtrees — as canonical `sceneformat` YAML onto the system clipboard (`core.Scope.SetClipboard`, or the injectable `Editor.WriteClipboard` hook), so they paste into another scene or a text editor. Cut removes the originals and their connections in one undoable step. When a 2D tile region selection is active under a tile tool, `mod+c` and `mod+x` continue to copy or cut the tile region instead.
- **Paste (`mod+v`, `Paste`, `PasteWith`, `PasteYAML`, `PasteYAMLWith`) and Paste as Child (`mod+shift+v`, `PasteInto`)**: parse scene-format YAML from the system clipboard (`core.Scope.ReadClipboard` / `Editor.ReadClipboard`) and place the objects on `ActiveLayer` (or under `ParentID`) at the hovered viewport pointer (`SetViewportPointer2D` / `SetViewportPointer3D`) or offset next to originals when IDs are already taken in the scene. Original IDs are kept when unused in the target scene and allocated fresh unique IDs when taken, rewriting internal connections and scene overrides (including instanced `"enemy_1/body"` paths) to the new IDs and clearing duplicate `current` flags on pasted 3D cameras. Every paste runs `PlaceObject`'s checks (`sceneformat.Validate`, `ValidateComponents`, `ValidateConnections`, `ValidatePhysicsLayers`, and `Resolve` for instanced scenes) and commits as **one undo step**; unparseable or invalid YAML is refused in the status bar without modifying the scene or history.
- **Duplicate (`mod+d`, `DuplicateObject`, `DuplicateObjects`, `DuplicateSelected`, `DuplicateSelectedObjects`)**: clones the selected object(s) next to the originals on the same layer and parent in **one undo step**, allocating unique IDs and rewriting internal connections and overrides to the copies without overwriting the system clipboard.
- **Group (`mod+g`, `GroupObjects`, `GroupSelected`) and Ungroup (`mod+shift+g`, `UngroupObject`, `UngroupSelected`)**: Group wraps the selection under a new empty parent placed at their common 2D/3D world centre without moving any object on screen (converting child local transforms via `keepWorldTransform`), in **one undo step**. Ungroup lifts direct children out into the parent's slot while preserving their world transforms in **one undo step**, removing an empty unconnected group container automatically while keeping non-empty parents intact.
## 3D navigation mesh baking (#901)

In 3D view mode, the editor can bake a Recast-style 3D navigation mesh directly from the open scene's static geometry (`navbake3d`) and preview it over the 3D viewport:

- **NavMesh bake panel** (`NavMeshPanelView`, toggled by **View > 3D NavMesh Bake...**, the Command Palette `CmdNavMeshPanel`, or `SetNavMeshPanelOpen`): configures the agent name, radius, height, step height (`MaxClimb`), maximum walkable slope (`MaxSlope`), voxel cell size (`CellSize`), cell height (`CellHeight`), and output `.navmesh.yaml` path (`NavMeshBakeConfig` / `SetNavMeshBakeConfig`). When a project with a `navigation` section is opened, the panel seeds its defaults from `game.yaml`.
- **Baking and scene link** (**Bake** button, `CmdBakeNavMesh3D`, or `BakeNavMesh3D`): collects static 3D geometry from the open scene's glTF models (including `modelNode` and `modelMesh`), built-in `mesh` primitives, static non-trigger `collider3d` shapes (skipping dynamic/kinematic `body3d` objects, triggers, and screen-space layers), plus any extra terrain heightmaps, `GridMap`s, or physics colliders supplied by `Editor.OnBakeNavMesh3DGeometry`. Writes a deterministic, byte-identical `.navmesh.yaml` atomically, updates `SceneDoc.NavMesh` as one undoable step (`SetSceneNavMesh`, also editable in the Scene Settings inspector), and loads the baked mesh for immediate viewport preview. Reopening a scene whose `navMesh` file exists on disk automatically loads it (`LoadNavMesh3D`).
- **3D viewport overlay** (3D viewport bar **Overlays** popover, **Overlay** panel switch, **View > Show 3D NavMesh**, Command Palette `CmdNavMeshOverlay`, or `ShowNavMesh3D` / `SetShowNavMesh3D`): draws translucent cyan walkable polygons, detail edges, and crisp polygon boundary outlines over the 3D scene viewport through `Camera3D`.

## Scene tabs, scene files and unsaved changes (#866, #873)

The editor keeps each open scene in its own tab in the tab bar above the viewport (`SceneTabsView`), so an author can edit an instanced or base scene beside the level that uses it without losing undo history:

- **Per-tab state**: each tab (`Tabs`, `ActiveTab`, `TabCount`, `SelectTab`, `SelectTabByID`) keeps its own `SceneDoc`, `BuiltScene`, `History`, selection (`SelectedID`), active layer and tile selection, 2D and 3D view transforms (`View2D`, `Camera3D`), scene-tree visibility/lock session flags, `OnSave` callback, and dirty marker (`TabDirty`). Switching tabs never clears any tab's undo history; undoing in one tab never changes another.
- **Opening and deduplicating tabs**: opening a scene from the file browser (a `*.scene.yaml` or `*.scene.yml` file, or one listed under `autoload.scenes`), the Problems panel, the Search and References panel, the Command Palette, `NewScene`, `OpenScene`, or `OpenFile` opens a new tab (replacing an untouched initial untitled tab) or switches to the existing tab when that scene is already open. **Open Instanced Scene** (`OpenInstancedScene`, available in the inspector for an instancing object, the scene tree context menu, the File menu, and the Command Palette) and **Open Base Scene** (`OpenBaseScene`, available in the Scene Settings inspector for an inheriting scene, the File menu, and the Command Palette) open the referenced scene in a tab or switch to it when already open.
- **Live instance and inheritance updates on save**: saving a scene (`Save`, `SaveFile`, `SaveSceneAs`, `SaveTab`, `SaveAll`) rebuilds every other open tab that instances or inherits scenes (`BuiltScene` and `Scene3D`), so instances and inherited objects show the change immediately without reopening those tabs. `MoveFile` rewrites paths and references across every open tab.
- **Shared project, plugins, Play, and closing tabs**: the project file (`game.yaml`), plugins, and Play mode stay shared across tabs, with plugins and Play acting on the active tab (tab switching and closing are disabled while Play runs). Closing a clean tab (`CloseTab`, `RequestCloseTab`, `CmdCloseTab` / `mod+w`, or the tab's close button) closes it immediately; closing a dirty tab prompts to Save (saving only that tab and rebuilding dependent tabs), Discard (discarding only that tab while keeping shared project edits intact), or Cancel. Closing the last remaining tab replaces it with a clean untitled scene.

The header bar's scene File menu (`toolbar-file`) holds New Scene, which writes a minimal valid scene (named after its file, as large as the project's display or window, with one world layer) to a path from the project's root (`NewScene`), Save, Save As, which saves the active scene under another path and makes that file the tab's (`SaveSceneAs`), Revert, which puts back the active scene as its file holds it (`RevertScene`), Open Instanced Scene, Open Base Scene, Settings, and Close Tab. A path without a `.yaml` or `.yml` extension gets `.scene.yaml` (one ending in `.scene` gets `.yaml`), the name `game check` and `game index` find scenes by. Neither New Scene nor Save As overwrites another file or writes a scene under the project's own file names, `game.yaml` and `nsgo.yaml`. Revert is one undoable step, so Undo brings the reverted edits back.

`SceneDirty` reports whether the active scene differs from what it was opened from or last saved to, comparing encodings as `ProjectDirty` does for `game.yaml` and `ClipDirty` does for the open animation clip, so undoing back to the saved state makes the scene, project or clip clean again; `TabDirty` checks a specific tab and `AnySceneDirty` reports whether any open tab is dirty. A `MoveFile` that rewrites an open scene's file leaves it as clean or dirty as it was; one that rewrites references in an open scene but not in its file, as for a scene `game mv` does not read (no `.scene.yaml` name and nothing names it), leaves the scene dirty so that saving it keeps the new paths. Each dirty tab displays a `*` marker, the header bar shows the active scene's breadcrumb and an unsaved dot (`toolbar-unsaved`) while any open tab, the project, open clip or another auxiliary document has unsaved edits (`HasUnsavedChanges`), and the window title follows (`WindowTitle`). `EditorView` owns the window title, so an editor built on the package, such as a game's own, names itself with `Editor.AppName` rather than setting the title, and the title ends with that name ("NSGo Scene Editor" while it is empty). **File > Save** in the header or desktop menu bar, the Command Palette's Save, and Mod+S (`Save`, the `CmdSave` command) save what was edited in the active scene and the shared project; an unedited file is left as written, comments included. Switching projects (`RequestOpenProject`), quitting the editor, and closing its window ask first while any open tab, the project or open clip has unsaved edits: Save saves all dirty tabs and the project (`SaveAll`) and goes on, Discard drops them and goes on, and Cancel keeps them and stays. Discarding the project's edits puts back what follows from the project, as undoing them does: an audition through an audio bus the edits added stops, for example. A forced quit, as at logout, cannot wait for an answer and does not ask.

### Reloading files changed on disk and save conflict guard (#906)

While a UI scope is attached, the editor watches the open scene (`FilePath`), the open animation clip (`ClipPath`), `game.yaml` (`ProjectPath`) and the project directory tree (`DiskWatchInterval`, default 250ms; `PollDiskChanges` performs an immediate check in Go):

- **Clean files reload automatically**: when an open scene, clip or `game.yaml` changes on disk without unsaved edits, the editor reloads it automatically as one undoable `History` step (batching multi-file git branch switches such as `Switched branch · main → boss-room · 2 files`), updates the saved snapshot so the file stays clean, and shows a dismissible reload notice (`DiskReloadNotice`) with object-level diff details (**Show diff**).
- **Dirty files never reload under the author**: when an open file with unsaved edits changes on disk, the editor keeps the in-memory edits intact and shows an external-change conflict bar (`DiskConflicts`, `PrimaryDiskConflict`) offering **Reload from disk** (`ReloadFromDisk`, keeping the author's edits in `History` as `"Unsaved edits before reload"`), **Keep mine** (`KeepLocalVersion`, keeping the disk version in `History` as `"Changed outside the editor"` and acknowledging the disk snapshot so a subsequent Save succeeds), and for scenes **Compare** (`SetDiskCompareOpen`, `SetSceneConflictChoice`, `ApplySceneConflictMerge`) to pick **Mine** or **Disk** per overlapping object while taking single-side changes automatically.
- **Save conflict guard**: `Save`, `SaveFile`, `SaveProject` and `SaveClipFile` compare the on-disk SHA-256 snapshot recorded at open or last save before writing and refuse to overwrite externally modified files with `ErrDiskConflict` (`*DiskConflictError`). In `EditorView`, hitting a conflict opens a confirmation dialog (**Cancel**, **Reload from disk**, or **Overwrite** via `ConfirmOverwriteSave` / `ForceSave`).
- **Automatic file browser refresh**: adding, removing or renaming files in the open project directory automatically updates the file browser (`FilesView`) and invalidates the project index and Problems check without pressing Refresh.

## Layers panel (#862)

The layers panel (`LayersView`), under the scene tree, lists the open scene's layers in draw order, the first drawn at the top, each with its space (`world` or `screen`) and its tilemap: the tileset and size of an inline grid (`tiles 16x12`), the name of the top-level tilemap it refers to, or the path of an external Tiled map. Pressing a layer's row makes it the active layer, which the Place tool and the tile tools work on, the tile palette shows and the status bar names, with `hidden` and `locked` after it when they apply; pressing a layer's row in the scene tree does the same. In Go, `Layers()` returns what the panel shows.

- **Add and remove**: the + button adds a world layer named `layer_N` that draws over every other layer and makes it active (`AddLayer(name, space)`, which takes the highest order of the layers the scene draws, those of a scene it inherits included, and puts the layer last in the file). The bin button removes the active layer (`RemoveLayer(name)`), with its objects and tilemap and with the scene's connections from or to its objects; Undo puts all of them back where they were, and the scene is saved byte for byte as before. When the active layer is removed, the layer that takes its place in the file, or the one before it, becomes active.
- **Rename**: a double press or the context menu's Rename edits the name in the row; Return or leaving the field keeps it, and Escape drops it (`RenameLayer(oldName, newName)`). An empty name and one another layer has are refused. The active layer and the layer's lock follow the new name.
- **Order**: drag a row onto the top or bottom half of another to put the layer before or after it, or use the context menu's Move Layer Up and Move Layer Down (`MoveLayer(name, index)`, where index is the position in the draw order). The layer moves in the file next to the layer it is to draw after or before, and its `order` changes only when the value it has would not draw it there, to that neighbour's value; the other layers keep their place and their orders.
- **Space**: the context menu's World Space and Screen Space set the layer's `space` (`SetLayerSpace(name, space)`); its objects keep their coordinates.
- **Visibility**: the eye button hides or shows the layer by writing its `visible` field (`SetLayerVisible(name, visible)`). Hiding writes `visible: false`; showing leaves the field unset, its default, so hiding and showing a layer gives back the same file. This is the scene's own setting, which the game reads too, and an undoable edit. A hidden layer is not picked in either viewport, and its objects are not moved there even when they are selected (no 3D gizmo, no Move-tool drag), as for an object hidden in the scene tree.
- **Lock**: the lock button locks the layer against the viewports for the session (`SetLayerLocked(name, locked)`): a press passes over its objects to what lies under them, its objects are not moved there (no 3D gizmo, no Move-tool drag), and the Place, Paint, Erase and Fill tools change nothing while it is the active layer and say so in the status bar. Like the scene tree's object lock, it is not an edit, is not undone, is never written to the scene file, and opening another scene clears it; the panels and the inspector still edit the layer and its objects.

In a scene that inherits another (`inherits`), a layer named like one of the base's extends it: its name and draw order belong to the base scene, so renaming or moving it, renaming a layer to a base layer's name, and adding one with a base layer's name are refused, while hiding it, changing its space, or editing its parallax and repeat records undoable entries under `layerOverrides` (with per-field Revert and Revert All in the inspector and layer context menu), and removing it removes only the objects the scene adds to it. Other scene files that inherit the open one and name its layers are not rewritten by a rename. Every layer edit that would leave a scene that does not validate, or does not resolve with the scenes it instances or inherits, is refused before it reaches the history.

## Animation editor

The editor's animation panel keys any registered property on a timeline and saves the clip as an [`animation` resource file](resources.md#animation), played in the game by [animclip](animclip.md) (an AnimationPlayer equivalent). The panel shows below the viewport while a clip is open.

- **Opening a clip**: the Command Palette's **Animate** command (`CmdAnimate`) starts a new one-second clip on the selected object. In Go, `NewClip(target, name, length)` does the same, `NewClipFromAsepriteTag(target, tag)` builds a clip matching an Aseprite tag's frames, exact durations and loop setting from the target object's atlas, `NewClipFromFrames(target, name, frames, fps, loop)` builds a flipbook clip from a run of atlas frames at a fixed frame rate, `OpenClipFile(path, target)` and `OpenClipYAML(data, target)` open a clip file, checked against the component registry (a misspelled property fails to open with the file name), and `CloseClip` closes it. The panel's Flipbook row also lets you pick an Aseprite tag (From Tag) or a frame rate (From Frames) directly when the target object has a sprite atlas. `go tool game editor scene.yaml clip.animation.yaml [object-id]` opens a scene and selects the object `object-id` (the scene's first object when it is left out), opens the clip on it when the file exists, and the panel's Save Clip button writes that file.
- **Keying**: pick a property (an object field such as `x`, `alpha` or `sprite.frame`, or a registered component field such as `body.speed`) and press Key to key the value it has in the scene at the playhead; `KeyProperty(target, property, at)` does the same. Choosing `sprite.frame` reveals a frame picker listing the target object's atlas frames (`ClipTargetFrames`) so you can key any frame directly (`KeySpriteFrame(target, frame, at)`). With another object selected, the key goes to a track that targets it. A track that names the clip's own object is the same track as one with an empty target, and a clip with two tracks driving the same property of one object is refused. A bool key holds its value until the next key, and `sprite.frame` always steps discretely without interpolation.
- **Editing keys**: each track is a lane of its keys; a `sprite.frame` track displays each keyed frame as a thumbnail cell spanning its duration (`ClipFrameEntry`). Press a key to select it and move the playhead to it, then edit its time and value/interpolation (`linear`, `smooth` or `step`) for numeric tracks, or its atlas frame (`SetKeyFrame`) and duration (`-10ms` / `+10ms`, `FrameDuration`, `SetFrameDuration`) for `sprite.frame` tracks, or delete it; `SetKeyValue`, `MoveKey`, `SetKeyInterp`, `DeleteKey` and `RemoveClipTrack` do the same in Go. Values are checked against the property: 0 or 1 for a bool, a whole number for an int, inside the field's range, and a frame name present in the target's atlas for `sprite.frame`.
- **Clip settings and events**: the panel edits the clip's name, length and loop (`SetClipName`, `SetClipLength`, `SetClipLoop`), and adds named events at the playhead (`AddClipEvent`, `DeleteClipEvent`).
- **Scrubbing and preview**: the scrubber moves the playhead (`SeekClip`; at the end of a looping clip it shows the last keys) and Play plays the clip on a `game.Loop` (`PlayClip`, `PauseClip`, or `StepClip` to step it by hand). The viewport, in 2D and 3D, then shows a copy of the scene posed by the clip with the same `animclip.Player` and `BindScene` the game uses, so the scene being edited never takes animated values and a clip plays the same in the game. A clip the scene cannot play, such as one whose track targets a deleted object, stays open and `ClipError` says why. Asynchronous clip loaders can toggle the panel's loading card with `ClipLoading` / `SetClipLoading`.
- **Undo**: every clip edit, opening and closing included, goes through the same undo/redo history as scene edits.

`SaveClipYAML` and `SaveClipFile` write deterministic YAML, so a clip that is opened and saved again does not change.

### Sprite state machine graph (#1054)

Opening a `*.states.yaml` file (from the file browser, command palette, or `OpenStatesFile` / `OpenStatesYAML` / `OpenStatesProjectFile`) opens the **State Machine** graph panel below the viewport:

- **Graph and active state**: every state appears as a node card showing its name, clip metadata (frame count, frame duration, loop/once) and sprite thumbnail, with directed labelled transition edges and an `Any state` wildcard badge. During preview, the active state node highlights live and the preview card renders the active clip frame.
- **Drive with keys**: toggling **Drive with keys** binds the preview to the project's input actions (such as `move_left`, `move_right`, `jump`, `attack`, `hurt`) through the same `animclip.InputDriver` and `animclip.StateMachine` the game uses, advancing on fixed steps on `game.Loop` so interactive preview and runtime playback match frame for frame.
- **Outgoing transitions (`LEAVING <STATE>`)**: selecting any state displays its outgoing transitions (including `Any state` interrupts) and their conditions, and graph edits (`AddStatesState`, `RemoveStatesState`, `AddStatesTransition`, `RemoveStatesTransition`, `SetStatesEntry`, `SetStatesNodePos`, `SetStatesDoc`) go through the editor's undo/redo history and save back to deterministic canonical YAML (`SaveStatesYAML` / `SaveStatesFile`).

## Mods, plugins and packs (#1268)

The engine has three ways to extend a game, and they are not interchangeable:

| | Editor (Go) plugin | Script mod | Library pack |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Written in | Go | TSX | YAML and media |
| Who writes it | the game's developers, third-party tool authors | players | anyone |
| When it loads | compiled into the editor binary | at run time, with hot reload (`Manager.Reload`) | installed into the project |
| Trust | full | sandboxed, capability-gated | carries no code |
| Platforms | all, iOS and the web included | native; the web only with `ManagerOptions.WebUnisolated` | all |
| Extends the editor | yes | no (today) | turns on built-in plugins only |

An editor plugin is an `editor.Plugin` the editor binary registers with `Editor.RegisterPlugin` and a project turns on by name under `editor.plugins` in `game.yaml`, as the next section describes. A script mod is a `mod.yaml` manifest plus a TSX entry script that `modding.Manager` runs in a sandbox limited to the capabilities the manifest asks for and the host grants; see [modding](modding.md). A library pack is a set of files `library.Install` writes into the project and records in `game.yaml`; see [Asset and Plugin Library](library.md). A pack may not hold Go source or module files (`.go`, `go.mod` and the like are refused), so a plugin pack only turns on editor plugins the editor binary was already built with — it never ships their code.

Which one do you need?

- A new panel, inspector, viewport tool or 3D gizmo in the editor, or component types the editor checks scenes against: an editor plugin, below.
- Let players change the shipped game, within limits the game sets: a script mod.
- Share sprites, scenes, sounds or a starter template: a library pack.

A Go plugin is compiled into the binary rather than loaded at run time because Go has no portable way to load code on every platform — iOS and the web cannot load native code at run time — so a plugin ships inside the editor command it extends.

## Editor plugins (#492, #898)

A plugin extends the scene editor with its own panels, an inspector for its own component types, 2D and 3D viewport tools, and per-component 3D gizmos, as Godot's `EditorPlugin` does. Go cannot load code at run time on every platform, so a plugin is Go code built into the editor binary: the binary registers it with `Editor.RegisterPlugin`, and a project turns it on by naming it in its [project file](project.md):

```yaml
editor:
  plugins: [health]
```

A registered plugin the open project does not name stays off, and the order of `editor.plugins` is the order its panels and tools appear in. `OpenProject` names in the status bar the plugins the project lists that this binary was built without (`MissingPlugins`). `go tool game editor` registers no plugins and no component types, so a game that has its own builds its own editor command. `go tool game generate editor` writes one into a game's `main` package, built with the `gameeditor` tag in place of the game, that calls `editorcmd.Main` with the game's `Components` and its plugins, and `go tool game editor` runs it on the project ([Command Line](cli.md#generate)).
