Package `editorcmd` is the command line both share: `New(Options)` builds an editor with the component types, plugins, name and library given and `ProjectAssets` set, checking scenes against the component types together with the built-in physics components (`body2d`, `collider2d`, `trigger2d` and their 3D forms) the registry does not register itself, `Open(ed, args)` opens a project, a scene or a clip as `go tool game editor` does, and `Main` and `Run` parse the command line, open the project in the working directory without arguments and show the window, 1120 by 680 points unless `Options.WindowWidth` and `Options.WindowHeight` set another size. An editor command written by hand calls `editorcmd.Main` the same way:

```go
// cmd/editor/main.go: the game's own editor command.
package main

func main() {
	editorcmd.Main(editorcmd.Options{
		Components:     health.Components(),
		Plugins:        []editor.Plugin{healthplugin.Plugin()},
		AppName:        "Field Medic Editor",
		RequirePlugins: true, // refuse a project that lists a plugin this command lacks
	})
}
```

An `editor.Plugin` has a `Name`, which follows the rule of other names in `game.yaml` (no spaces or control characters), and lists of extensions:

- **Panels** (`Panel`): a view docked left of the viewport (`DockLeft`, after the asset browser), right of the inspector (`DockRight`) or below the viewport (`DockBottom`), under its `Title`. `View(cx, ed, rev)` builds it once when it appears, and the panel stays mounted, with its state, while its plugin is on, however other panels come and go; it reads `rev` inside reactive functions, as the editor's own panels do, to follow every change to the editor's state. A dock without panels takes no space.
- **Inspectors** (`Inspector`): the inspector draws the component `Component` names with `View(cx, ed, rev, objectID)` below the component's title, in place of its generic fields. `Editor.ComponentField` reads a field, with its registered default when the scene leaves it unset, and reports false for an object without the component. Like the generic fields, the inspector's controls should be disabled while `Playing` reports true, and an edit the editor refuses should go to the status bar with `Editor.Report`. When two plugins that are on draw the same component, the one listed first in `editor.plugins` wins.
- **Tools** (`PluginTool`): a custom tool listed in **View > Tools** and the **Command Palette** (`⌘K`), active in 2D and/or 3D depending on which hooks the tool implements. While it is the active tool (`SetPluginTool`; `Tool` is then `ToolPlugin`):
  - **2D hooks**: a press on the 2D viewport calls `Press(ed, x, y)` with canvas coordinates (`CanvasToWorld` converts them to the world, `CanvasToScreen` to a screen-space layer), drags call `OnDragBegin(ed, x, y)` (returning `true` to claim the drag), `OnDragMove(ed, x, y)`, `OnDragEnd(ed, x, y)` and `OnDragCancel(ed)` (`BeginPluginDrag2D`, `UpdatePluginDrag2D`, `EndPluginDrag2D`, `CancelPluginDrag2D`, `PluginDragging2D`), pointer motion calls `OnHover(ed, x, y, active)` (`SetPluginHover2D`, `ClearPluginHover2D`), and `Draw`, when set, draws over the 2D scene in edit mode.
  - **3D hooks (#898)**: a press on the 3D viewport calls `Press3D(ed, ray)` with the camera's world-space `math3d.Ray` (`PickRay3D`), drags call `OnDragBegin3D(ed, ray)` (returning `true` to claim the drag instead of orbiting the camera), `OnDragMove3D(ed, ray)`, `OnDragEnd3D(ed, ray)` and `OnDragCancel3D(ed)` (`BeginPluginDrag3D`, `UpdatePluginDrag3D`, `EndPluginDrag3D`, `CancelPluginDrag3D`, `PluginDragging3D`), pointer motion calls `OnHover3D(ed, ray, active)` (`SetPluginHover3D`, `ClearPluginHover3D`), and `Draw3D(ed, d, proj)` draws over the 3D viewport using `Projection3D` (`Camera`, `Width`, `Height`, `ViewProj`, `Project`, `ProjectSegment`, `ScreenRay`, `GizmoLength`, `DrawPoint`, `DrawLine`, `DrawBox`).
  - A tool must provide at least one of `Press`, `OnDragBegin`, `Press3D`, or `OnDragBegin3D`. Tools with only 2D hooks stay idle in 3D; tools with 3D hooks activate 3D view automatically when run from the Command Palette or **View > Tools**. Choosing another tool, or turning the plugin off, ends it.
- **3D Component Gizmos** (`Gizmos3D []ComponentGizmo3D`, #898): registers per-component-type 3D handles in object space (`GizmoHandlePoint3D`, `GizmoHandleLine3D`, `GizmoHandleBox3D`, optionally `DisplayOnly`) returned by `Handles(ed, objectID)` and an interactive `Drag(ed, drag GizmoDrag3D) error` function. The editor transforms handles from object space through the object's full ancestor hierarchy, hit-tests them in screen space (`PickComponentGizmo3D`, `BeginComponentGizmoDrag3D`, `UpdateComponentGizmoDrag3D`, `EndComponentGizmoDrag3D`, `CancelComponentGizmoDrag3D`), and passes `GizmoDrag3D` (with world and object-space rays plus `AxisDelta`, `PlanePoint`, `PlaneDelta`, and `ViewPlaneDelta` helpers). Edits written inside `Drag` through `Editor` (such as `SetComponentField` or `Editor.History`) preview live on every pointer move, roll back cleanly on `Escape` or cancel without polluting history, and commit as **one undo step** when the drag ends. When two active plugins register a 3D gizmo for the same component, the first in `editor.plugins` wins. The editor's built-in 3D transform gizmo (`Translate`, `Rotate`, `Scale`) and built-in `collider3d` / `trigger3d` handles share this same 3D handle API.

A plugin changes the scene only through the editor's methods, such as `SetComponentField`, `PlaceObject` and `MoveObject`, or by executing its own `Command` on `Editor.History`, so every edit it makes is undone and redone with the rest. `History.Group(label, fn)` records the edits `fn` makes as one step that one Undo takes back, and when `fn` fails it undoes them and records nothing; the editor's MCP server ([mcp](mcp.md#editor-tools)) applies each agent request that way. `RegisterPlugin` refuses a plugin without a name or with a name already registered, a panel, inspector or tool missing its ID, title, label, component or function, an ID or component name with control characters, an unknown dock and two panels, inspectors or tools with the same ID or component. Test IDs join the plugin name and the ID with `-` (`plugin-panel-<plugin>-<id>`, `tool-<plugin>-<id>`, `inspector-plugin-<plugin>-<component>`), so plugins used together should have names and IDs that do not join to the same string.

`SetPluginEnabled(name, on)` adds a registered plugin to `editor.plugins` or removes it, as one undoable edit of the project that **File > Save** (`mod+s`) writes; removing the last plugin removes the `editor` section when nothing else is left in it, including an `editor: {}` the author wrote, and Undo puts it back. `RegisteredPlugins`, `ActivePlugins` and `PluginEnabled` report which plugins the binary has and which are on.

### Shipping an editor with plugins

A game ships its editor as one more command in its own module, beside the game, and runs it with `go run ./cmd/editor` from the project directory. Keep the plugin in a package of its own that only the editor command imports, so the shipped game does not link the editor, and the component types in a package that both import, so the game and the editor check scenes against the same fields:

| Package | Imports | Holds |
| --- | --- | --- |
| the component package | `sceneformat` | the component types (`ComponentType`), a registry for `ParseWith` and `SetComponentRegistry`, and helpers that read a component with its defaults (`ComponentRegistry.Resolve`) |
| the plugin package | `editor` and the component package | `Plugin()`, returning the `editor.Plugin` |
| the game (`main`) | the component package | loads its scenes with `sceneformat.ParseWith` and the registry |
| `cmd/editor` | `editor`, the component and plugin packages | registers the component types and the plugin, then opens the project |

`game.yaml` names the plugin under `editor.plugins`, so the plugin is on whenever the game's editor opens the project. An editor command that should not start without its plugins sets `editorcmd.Options.RequirePlugins`, or checks `MissingPlugins` after `OpenProject` and fails when it is not empty. `go tool game editor` on a project with a `cmd/editor` main package runs that command with `go run ./cmd/editor`, as it runs the one `game generate editor` writes, and passes it the same command line, `-library` and a project, scene file, clip and object ID, so a `cmd/editor` should parse it with `editorcmd.Main` or `editorcmd.Run`. A `cmd/editor` that does not import `editorcmd` is not run: `go tool game editor` says so and opens the project itself.

[Field Medic](https://github.com/nsgo-dev/game/tree/main/examples/fieldmedic) (`examples/fieldmedic`) is a runnable game laid out this way. Its patients carry a `health` component (package `health`), and its `cmd/editor` registers the `health` plugin (package `healthplugin`): a Ward panel docked right of the inspector that lists every patient's hit points, with a Heal All button that heals them all in one undoable step it executes on `Editor.History`; an inspector for the `health` component with hit point and max fields and a bar of what is left; and an Admit tool that places a patient where the viewport is pressed and outlines every patient while it is active. Its tests open the game's own project in the editor and drive the panel, the inspector and the tool. The editor's own tests hold a smaller `health` plugin as well (`pkg/editor/plugin_test.go`).

## Library panel (#493)

With `Editor.Library` set to a pack library (`library.Open`), as `go tool game editor -library <index>` sets it, a panel beside the asset browser lists every pack in the library, with its kind, version, description and the version the open project has installed. Its Install, Update and Remove buttons call `InstallPack` and `RemovePack`, which write the pack's files beside `game.yaml`, record the pack and the autoload entries and plugins it adds in the project, and save the project; a registered plugin a plugin pack names turns on at once. They refuse while playing, without a project and while the project has unsaved edits (`ErrProjectDirty`), and they empty the undo history, since the files they write cannot be undone. A pack from a library over https is fetched off the event loop, with its button busy until it arrives (`FetchingPack`). The library format and what an install writes are in [Asset and Plugin Library](library.md).

## Unified Settings pages: Agent, Project, Input map, Audio buses, Export, General, and Shortcuts (#907, #908, #909, #916, #1028, #1060, #1061)

All per-computer agent controls, project-level `game.yaml` settings, and editor preferences share one unified **Settings** workspace (`SettingsView`, opened with `OpenSettingsPage(page)`, inspected with `SettingsOpen()` / `SettingsPage()`, and closed with `CloseSettings()` / `Escape`) laid out from `design/editor/Settings.dc.html` and `Spec.dc.html` using `theme.Palette` tokens in both Dark and Light appearances. The left sidebar groups **`AGENT`** (**Models and connections**, **Tools**, **Usage and privacy** — all per-computer and **You only**), **`PROJECT · game.yaml`** (**Project**, **Input map**, **Audio buses**, **Export**), and **`EDITOR · THIS COMPUTER`** (**General**, **Shortcuts**); each row displays its label, help text, value control, and agent-policy badge (**Agent may change** or **You only**), and every interactive control across all pages uses real focusable `ui.Button` / `ui.*` widgets reachable via `Tab` with visible focus rings.

### Agent pages: Models and connections, Tools, and Usage and privacy (#1028, #1030)

Stored per computer in `EditorSettings` (`editor.yaml` under `agent`, never inside the project) with every mutator refused when invoked by an agent (`ErrAgentSettingsYouOnly`):

- **Models and connections (`SettingsPageModels`)**: shows connected local MCP agents (such as Claude Code), the built-in API connection (`Anthropic API`, whose key is stored exclusively in NSGo's `core.Secrets` under `AgentAPIKeySecretAccount` / the host OS keychain under `AgentKeychainServiceName` via `StoreAgentAPIKey`, `HasAgentAPIKey`, `DeleteAgentAPIKey`, and `AgentAPIKey` — never written to `editor.yaml`, project files, or logs), **+ Connect another agent or model** (opening `ConnectAgentDialogView`), and **WHO DOES WHAT** task routing (`AgentTaskRoute` / `SetAgentTaskRoute` / `ResolveAgentModelRoute`). The sidebar meta reports the active connection count (`AgentActiveConnectionCount`, e.g. `2 on`).
- **Built-in agent runner (`SubmitAgentTaskPrompt`, `Editor.AgentTools`, `Editor.BuiltInAgentEndpoint`)**: routes each task kind (`AgentTaskSceneEdit`, `AgentTaskPlaytest`, `AgentTaskProblems`, `AgentTaskAskInPlace`, `AgentTaskArtSound`) to either a connected local MCP agent or the built-in Anthropic Messages API runner (`DefaultBuiltInModel` / `DefaultBuiltInFastModel`), validating that custom endpoints use `https://` (or loopback `http://` for local tests) and never forward `x-api-key` across redirect hosts. `editormcp.New` wires `Editor.AgentTools` automatically so built-in tool calls execute through the same `editormcp.Server` tool toggles, permission rules, `WithAgentActor` You-only guard, and symlink-aware outbound privacy enforcement as external MCP clients.
- **Weekly limit queueing & model error failover (`AgentStatusLimitReached`, `AgentQueuedRequests`, `AgentModelErrorState`)**: reaching the person-only weekly request limit (`WeeklyRequestLimit` / `SetWeeklyRequestLimit`) transitions the Agent header chip to `Limit reached · resets 00:00` with a **Usage** link and queues subsequent requests (`SendQueuedAgentRequest`, `RemoveQueuedAgentRequest`) until `CheckAgentUsageReset` crosses the weekly reset or the person raises the limit. Transient model errors (`429`, `5xx`, `529` overloaded) retry on `AgentModelRetryDelays` (`4 s, 8 s, 16 s, 32 s`), rendering the model error card with **Retry now** (`RetryAgentTurn`) and **Use `<connected agent>`** (`SwitchAgentTurnToAlternative`) to update WHO DOES WHAT and run the request on another connected agent without double-counting usage.
- **Tools (`SettingsPageTools`)**: lists every tool registered on the editor's MCP server (`RegisteredMCPTools`) with its monospace name, description, and an accessible `role="switch"` toggle (`IsMCPToolEnabled` / `SetMCPToolEnabled`), showing `"enabled / total"` in the sidebar (`EnabledMCPToolCount`). `game_delete_file` is off by default; turning any tool off removes it from the MCP server's client tool list (`tools/list_changed`) and returns a readable refusal if called. Below the editor's tools, **OTHER MCP SERVERS THE AGENT MAY USE** starts with **Running game · nsgo dev** (`nsgo-game` / `go tool nsgo mcp`) and provides **+ Add a server** (`AddAgentMCPServer`, `SetAgentMCPServerEnabled`, `RemoveAgentMCPServer`), syncing enabled servers into the open project's `.mcp.json` and `.codex/config.toml` (`editormcp.InstallWithServers`).
- **Usage and privacy (`SettingsPageUsage`)**: displays **This week** requests against a user-configured weekly limit (`WeeklyRequestLimit` / `SetWeeklyRequestLimit` / `RecordAgentRequest`), **Kept** share of reviewed agent changes (`RecordAgentReviewOutcome`), and local **Playtests** run count (`RecordPlaytestRun`), followed by the four **WHAT MAY LEAVE THIS COMPUTER** switches (`AgentPrivacy` / `SetAgentPrivacy`): **Scene and data files** (on), **Viewport images** (on), **Go source code** (off), and **Art and audio files** (off). The editor's MCP server refuses, with the reason, any tool call or resource read whose result would carry switched-off content for every connected agent. Files that look like credentials or the editor's private state never leave, whatever the switches say: `.env`, `.env.*` and `*.env`, `.envrc`, `*.pem`, `*.key`, `*.p8`, `*.p12`, `*.pfx`, `*.jks`, `*.keystore`, `keystore.properties`, `.netrc`, `.npmrc`, `.git-credentials`, `credentials.json`, `google-services.json`, `GoogleService-Info.plist`, `service-account*.json`, SSH keys named `id_*`, and anything inside a `.git`, `.nsgo`, `.aws` or `.ssh` folder. The match ignores case, including Unicode case folding and the names a case-insensitive file system stores (such as NTFS short names), and follows in-project symbolic links; every tool that reads, edits, renames or deletes a file by its path refuses them with "looks like a credential or the editor's private state; the editor never sends it to an agent", the `@`-mention picker does not offer them, and a file chip never carries them. Keep other secrets out of files an agent may read, or turn **Scene and data files** off.

### Project page (#907, #1060)

While a project is open, **Project > Project Settings**, `mod+shift+,`, or the Command Palette (`CmdProjectSettings`, `ProjectSettingsOpen` / `SetProjectSettingsOpen`, or `OpenSettingsPage(SettingsPageProject)`) opens the **Project** page in `SettingsView` (`ProjectSettingsPanelView`) to edit `game.yaml` exclusively through `EditProject`, so every change is validated by `project.Validate`, participates in Undo/Redo, and writes both `game.yaml` and the synced `nsgo.yaml` beside it on **Save** (`SaveProject`):

- **General**: edits `name`, `id`, `window` width/height, `autoload.mainScene`, and `appVersion` directly on the overview page (`SetProjectName`, `SetProjectID`, `SetProjectWindowSize`, `SetProjectMainScene`, `SetProjectAppVersion`).
- **Window**: edits `window.title`, `width`/`height`, `minWidth`/`minHeight`/`maxWidth`/`maxHeight` limits, `resizable`, and `appearance` (`SetProjectWindowTitle`, `SetProjectWindowSize`, `SetProjectWindowLimits`, `SetProjectWindowResizable`, `SetProjectWindowAppearance`).
- **Display**: toggles the optional `display` section (`SetProjectDisplayEnabled`) and edits its virtual `width`/`height`, `mode` (`fit`, `fill`, `stretch`, `expand`), `integerScale`, `pixelSnap`, and `barColor` (`SetProjectDisplaySize`, `SetProjectDisplayMode`, `SetProjectDisplayIntegerScale`, `SetProjectDisplayPixelSnap`, `SetProjectDisplayBarColor`).
- **Autoload**: selects `autoload.mainScene` (`SetProjectMainScene`) and adds, updates, or removes `autoload.scenes` and `autoload.resources` entries (`AddProjectAutoloadScene`, `UpdateProjectAutoloadScene`, `RemoveProjectAutoloadScene`, `AddProjectAutoloadResource`, `UpdateProjectAutoloadResource`, `RemoveProjectAutoloadResource`) with searchable file pickers populated from the open project's files (`ProjectSceneFiles`, `ProjectResourceFiles`).
- **Physics**: edits `physics.gravity2D`, `physics.gravity3D`, `physics.solverIterations2D` / `solverIterations3D`, and named collision `physics.layers` (`SetProjectPhysicsGravity2D`, `SetProjectPhysicsGravity3D`, `SetProjectPhysicsSolverIterations`, `AddProjectPhysicsLayer`, `RemoveProjectPhysicsLayer`).
- **Editor plugins**: shows every registered plugin (`RegisteredPlugins`) with a toggle switch backed by `SetPluginEnabled`, plus any plugins listed in `game.yaml` that this editor binary lacks (`MissingPlugins`) with an option to remove them.

Any value `project.Validate` refuses displays its validation error inline on the offending row without mutating the project or history. Before the first save of the open project (`ProjectCanonicalSavePending`), the page displays a notice explaining that saving writes `game.yaml` in canonical YAML form, which does not preserve comments.

### Audio buses page (#909, #1060)

While a project is open, **Project > Audio Bus Mixer**, the Command Palette (`CmdAudioMixer`), or **Settings › Audio buses** (`AudioMixerOpen` / `SetAudioMixerOpen`, or `OpenSettingsPage(SettingsPageAudio)`) opens the **Audio buses** Settings page (`AudioMixerPanelView`) to edit `game.yaml`'s `audio` section directly through `EditProject`, so every edit validates against `project.Validate`, participates in the editor's Undo/Redo history, and writes cleanly on **Save** (`SaveProject`):

- **Master and buses**: adjust the master volume slider with dB readout (`SetAudioMasterVolume`), add new buses (`AddAudioBus`), rename (`RenameAudioBus`), delete (`DeleteAudioBus`), reorder (`MoveAudioBus`), and adjust each bus's routing (`Into Master`) and volume slider (`SetAudioBusVolume`). Deleting the last bus when no master volume is set removes the empty `audio` section, and Undo restores it.
- **Per-bus DSP effect chains**: add (`AddAudioBusEffect` / `AddAudioBusEffectKind`), remove (`RemoveAudioBusEffect`), reorder (`MoveAudioBusEffect`), and edit (`SetAudioBusEffect`) all six `project.Effect` types (`lowPass`, `highPass`, `delay`, `reverb`, `gain`, `bitcrush`). Numeric inputs bound parameters to `project.Validate`'s ranges (with `ClampAudioEffect`), and Go API calls refuse out-of-range values without altering the project or history.
- **Live audition**: choose any audio file from the open project (`ProjectAudioFiles`, `SelectAudioFile`) and press **Audition** (`AuditionAudioBus`, `StopAudition`, `SetAuditionLoop`) to play it through the selected bus on an editor-owned `sound.Mixer` configured with `project.File.ConfigureMixer`. Volume changes apply live to the playing stream without restarting it, and effect chain edits or Undo/Redo immediately re-process through the same `sound.Mixer` bus chain that `Music.Play` and `Mixer.LoadEffect` apply at run time. That chain runs on in-memory 16-bit PCM WAV sources only: a shipped game plays other formats without it (logging once per group), and auditioning one on a bus with effects reports the WAV decode error instead.

### Input map page (#908, #1060)

While a project is open, the Command Palette (`CmdInputMap`) or **Settings › Input map** (`InputMapOpen` / `SetInputMapOpen`, or `OpenSettingsPage(SettingsPageInput)`) opens the **Input map** Settings page (`InputMapPanelView`) to edit `game.yaml`'s `input` section directly through `EditProject` with full Undo/Redo and validation against `project.Validate`:

- **Action overview & editor**: lists each configured action with its keyboard, gamepad, and touch triggers alongside a button that jumps directly into editing that action. Add (`AddInputAction`), rename (`RenameInputAction`), and delete (`DeleteInputAction`) actions, and add (`AddInputBinding`), edit (`SetInputBinding`), or remove (`RemoveInputBinding`) bindings of every `game/input` kind: `key`, `keyCode`, `gamepadButton`, `mouseButton`, `touchRegion`, and `mouseRegion`. Renaming an action atomically updates every axis in `input.axes` that reads it so references stay consistent across Rename, Undo, and Redo.
- **Key capture**: pressing **Press a key** (`BeginKeyCapture` with `project.KindKey`) or **Press code** (`project.KindKeyCode`) enters capture mode and binds the next hardware key press (`CaptureKeyEvent`) directly to the selected action, with **Cancel** (`CancelKeyCapture`) to back out without changing the project.
- **Regions over virtual display size**: the region canvas scales to the project's `display` resolution (falling back to `window` when `display` is unset; `InputMapDisplaySize`), draws all `touchRegion` and `mouseRegion` bounds, and lets you drag existing regions to move them, drag a selected region's bottom-right handle to resize it, or drag on empty space to draw a new region (`BeginRegionDrag` / `UpdateRegionDrag` / `EndRegionDrag` / `SetInputRegion`). Each drag commits as one undoable `EditProject` step.
- **Axes, motion axes and gamepad sticks**: the **Axes** tab edits action axes (`AddInputAxis`, `SetInputAxis`, `DeleteInputAxis`), device motion sensor axes (`AddInputMotionAxis`, `SetInputMotionAxis`, `DeleteInputMotionAxis`), and controller analog stick axes (`AddInputGamepadAxis`, `SetInputGamepadAxis`, `DeleteInputGamepadAxis`). `BuildInputActions` binds the open project's map into a live `*input.Actions` via `project.File.BindInput`.

## Localization panel (#917)

The Localization panel (`LocalizationPanelView`) edits per-locale YAML string tables side by side below the viewport, with message keys as rows and project locales as columns.

- **Opening**: choose **Project > Localization Strings** or the Command Palette's **Localization Strings** command (`CmdLocalization`, which opens `<project>/locales` when a project is open, or starts an in-memory `"en"` table otherwise), or call `OpenLocalizations(dir)` / `NewLocalizations(dir, locales...)` in Go. `CloseLocalizations` closes the panel.
- **Keys and locales**: add or remove locale columns (`AddLocalizationLocale`, `RemoveLocalizationLocale`) and message key rows (`AddLocalizationKey`, `RemoveLocalizationKey`), normalizing locale tags to canonical BCP-47 form (for example `zh_hans` -> `zh-Hans`).
- **Simple strings and CLDR plurals**: toggle a key as plural (`SetLocalizationKeyPlural`) to edit all six CLDR categories (`zero`, `one`, `two`, `few`, `many`, `other`) via `SetLocalizationPlural` or `SetLocalizationPluralForm`, or edit simple strings via `SetLocalizationString`.
- **Missing translations**: cells whose translation is empty (or whose plural `other` fallback is empty) are highlighted with a warning border and `Missing` indicator; `LocalizationMissing`, `LocalizationMissingCount`, and `LocalizationIsMissing` report them programmatically. Untranslated keys are omitted when saved so runtime `l10n.Catalog` lookups fall back along the BCP-47 chain.
- **Undo, saving, and panel states**: every localization edit goes through `Editor.History` (Undo/Redo). **Save Strings** (`SaveLocalizations` / `SaveLocalizationsTo`) writes each locale's canonical YAML file to `<dir>/<locale>.yaml` using `l10n.EncodeYAML` and removes files for locales that were deleted from the workspace. Panel errors are surfaced in a dismissible banner (`LocalizationError` / `ClearLocalizationError`), and asynchronous loaders can toggle the loading state via `LocalizationLoading` / `SetLocalizationLoading`.

## Problems panel and live agent-proposed fixes (#913, #1036)

While a project is open, the Problems panel (`ProblemsView`) below the viewport runs `assetcheck.CheckWith` (using the editor's component registry) and `project.Check` over the project directory overlaid with every open, unsaved editor document off the UI goroutine, and reports the live problem count in the status bar (`Problems: N`). It re-checks automatically (debounced via `ProblemCheckDebounce`, defaulting to 80 ms) after every edit in the editor—made by the person or the agent—without waiting for a save, as well as when a project opens (`OpenProject`), after saving (`SaveFile`, `SaveProject`, `SaveClipFile`), after moving files (`MoveFile`, including undo and redo), and when the component registry changes (`SetComponentRegistry`). The panel's Check button (`CheckProblems`) re-runs the check on demand after edits made outside the editor.

Each row displays a problem's `file:line:column` location (`assetcheck.Problem.Location`, carrying the 1-based column wherever the scene or YAML format knows it), a Spec alert icon, and the message, matching `go tool game check` on the same project plus any project-level issues reported by `project.Check` (such as an `nsgo.yaml` out of sync with `game.yaml`, a missing installed pack file, or an invalid non-`.scene.yaml` autoloaded scene). Pressing a row (`OpenProblem`) opens the scene in a tab without discarding unsaved edits in other tabs, or selects a non-scene file in the file browser (`SelectedFile`, `SelectFile`). Problems attributed to objects in the open scene also draw directly on live objects in the 2D viewport using the Spec Problem overlay (2 px solid problem outline plus a filled alert pill with icon and message, toggled via `SetProblemOverlayVisible`) and show a problem count badge on the object's row in the scene tree (`ObjectProblems`, `ObjectProblemCount`).

- **Agent-proposed fixes (`ProposeProblemFix`, `ApplyProposedFix`, `ProblemAgentPanelView`)**: an agent inspecting live problems through MCP (`game_check`) can propose a fix via `problem_propose_fix` (providing the problem, an explanation, and a validated structured edit). Proposing a fix simulates the edit on a cloned scene document to verify validity and compute the exact `-`/`+` YAML diff, opens the right-hand **Agent** panel showing the problem card (`file:line:col`), explanation, diff, **Apply fix**, **Show me**, **Ignore**, and **"Ask about this problem…"**, and **never applies the edit or mutates any file**. Only the person pressing **Apply fix** (`ApplyProposedFix`) applies the change as a single undoable `History` step with a Spec toast and **Undo** action, so one Undo restores the previous YAML and brings the problem back.
- **Show me, Ignore, and Ask about this problem (`ShowProblem`, `IgnoreProblem`, `AskAboutProblem`)**: **Show me** opens the problem's scene, selects and frames the offending object, and highlights the exact YAML line. **Ignore** hides that problem from the Problems panel, status bar, scene tree badge, and viewport overlay until its file changes again (tracked by live content hash); any subsequent edit to that file evicts the ignore entry so the problem reappears automatically if it still holds. Submitting **"Ask about this problem…"** delivers the question with the problem, attributed object, and proposed fix attached (`OnAskAboutProblem`).

In Go, `Problems`, `ProblemCount`, `ProblemErr`, `CheckingProblems`, `WaitForProblems`, `IgnoreProblem`, `IsProblemIgnored`, `ProposeProblemFix`, `ProposedFixes`, `ApplyProposedFix`, `ShowProblem`, and `AskAboutProblem` manage live problems and proposed fixes safely.

## Export and release builds (#916, #1060)

While a project is open, **File > Export & Release...**, `mod+shift+e`, the Command Palette (`CmdExport`), or **Settings › Export** (`ExportPanelOpen` / `SetExportPanelOpen`, or `OpenSettingsPage(SettingsPageExport)`) opens the **Export** Settings page (`ExportPanelView`) in `SettingsView` to edit `game.yaml`'s `export` section and run release builds directly from the editor:

- **Presets, readiness, and output directory**: edit `export.output` (`SetExportOutput`) with inline validation and add, remove, or reorder per-platform presets (`AddExportPreset`, `RemoveExportPreset`, `MoveExportPreset`) with targets chosen strictly from each platform's valid `project.TargetsOf(platform)` (`SetExportPresetTargets`, `ToggleExportPresetTarget`). Each preset row displays its readiness (`ExportPresetReadiness`: **Ready** or **Set up** when signing/team configuration or credits are missing) and policy badge, and the **Export** sidebar item surfaces a warning badge (`ExportNavWarningMeta`) whenever a preset needs setup. Every change goes through `EditProject` (`project.Validate` + Undo/Redo), clears an empty `export` section automatically, and writes canonical YAML plus the synced `nsgo.yaml` on **Save** (`SaveProject`), matching `game project targets`.
- **Credits pre-flight**: whenever the panel opens, refreshes (`CheckExportCredits`), or starts a build, it runs the same `tooling/credits` scan that `game release` uses. Any asset without a credit is listed with its expected `*.import.yaml` sidecar path and blocks building until credited; once credited, refreshing clears the warning and successful builds write `CREDITS.md` beside the packages.
- **Builds, dry runs, and Output stream**: build all configured presets or toggle specific targets (`ExportSelectedTargets`), optionally selecting a distribution channel (`SetExportChannel`), release notes file (`SetExportReleaseNotes`), or **Dry Run** (`SetExportDryRun`). `StartExportBuild` / `RunExportBuild` share the release command planner with `go tool game release`, so dry-run builds stream the exact same `nsgo build` command line into the panel's Output view off the UI goroutine, and successful non-dry-run builds open the resolved output folder (`OpenOutputFolder` / `OpenExportOutputFolder`).

### Build and release screen (#1048, #1050)

For full release preparation, **Project > Build & Release...** (`CmdBuildRelease`, shortcut `mod+shift+r`, or `OpenBuildReleaseScreen` / `CloseBuildReleaseScreen` / `BuildReleaseScreenOpen`) opens the full-window `BuildReleaseScreenView` styled with Spec tokens:

- **Header and pre-build checks**: shows `"Release · Version <ver> from <commit> on <branch> · last release <version>"` with an editable version field (`SetReleaseVersion`), Dry Run toggle (`SetReleaseDryRun`), and **Build** / **Stop** / **Resume** controls (`StartReleaseBuild`, `StopReleaseBuild`, `ResumeReleaseBuild`). **BEFORE EVERY BUILD** summarizes `nsgo check` (problem count and elapsed time via `RefreshBuildPreflight`), the smoke playtest, and release budgets declared under `export.budgets` in `game.yaml` (`qualityProfile`, `downloadSizeMB`, `frameTimeP95Ms`, defaulting to Low profile / 80 MB / 16.7 ms).
- **Export presets, ordered steps, and logs**: lists each preset with an include checkbox (`SetBuildPresetIncluded`), platform summary, live status (`Queued`, `Building`, `Notarizing`, `Done`, `Failed`, `Set up`), artifact size, and delta against the last release (`SetLastReleaseBaseline`). Selecting a running preset shows the 6 ordered steps (`Check project`, `Compile Go`, `Pack assets`, `Sign`, `Smoke playtest`, `Measure`) with elapsed times, exact `releaseplan` commands matching `game release -dry-run`, and its streaming log (`build/<platform>.log`). Presets build one at a time; **Stop** keeps completed steps and **Resume** continues from the interrupted step.
- **Failure isolation and agent preset fixes**: if a preset fails (for example, a missing signing keystore at `Sign`), the screen shows the error, step and elapsed time, failing commands, and `"Other platforms keep building. Nothing was uploaded."` while continuing remaining checked presets. An agent-proposed preset fix diff (`SetBuildPresetFixProposal`) can be applied and rebuilt in one click (`ApplyBuildPresetFixAndRebuild`).
- **Finished results and platform readiness**: completed presets show the artifact path, size and delta against the last release, a `code` / `textures` / `audio` / `other` breakdown highlighting what grew, download-size and frame-time p95 budget Met/Missed badges, smoke playtest summary, **Show in Finder** (`ShowBuildArtifactInFinder`), **Edit preset**, and (for Web builds) a local test-link QR card. Platforms that cannot build yet (such as iOS) open the **READINESS** checklist (`EvaluateBuildReadiness`, backed by the shared `nsgo.yaml` checks: `id`, `icon.apple` / `icon.png` or default `assets/AppIcon.icon` / `assets/Icon.png`, `sign.identity`, `sign.profile`, `apple.privacy` and `resources/PrivacyInfo.xcprivacy` merged with NSGo's built-in privacy manifest, and `*.touch.yaml` layouts or `game.yaml` `touchRegion` bindings) clearly separating what the agent can handle (`RunBuildReadinessAction` / `RunBuildReadinessAgentSetup`, such as syncing `nsgo.yaml`, writing `resources/PrivacyInfo.xcprivacy`, or generating `controls.touch.yaml` from `game.yaml`'s existing actions) from steps that require the person (provisioning profile and Apple signing credentials).
- **Release notes drafted from commits and kept reviews (#1050)**: **RELEASE NOTES** reads git commits since the last release tag (or initial commit when untagged), kept review summaries (`SetReleaseKeptReviews` / `AddReleaseKeptReview`), and `changelog.d/*.md` fragments to draft player-facing Markdown (`DraftReleaseNotes`, `RedraftReleaseNotes`, `DefaultReleaseNotesDraft`, pluggable via `Editor.ReleaseNotesDrafter`, and `release_notes_draft` in `editormcp`), displaying `"Drafted from N commits since <tag/ref>"`. Editing the notes in place (`SetReleaseNotesText` / `SaveReleaseNotes`) saves directly and atomically to the exact Markdown file read by `game release -release-notes` (`ReleaseNotesFile` / `SetReleaseNotesFile`, defaulting to `release-notes.md`), modifying only that file.
- **Store uploads that always ask first and system keychain credentials (#1050, #1762)**: pressing **Review uploads…** (`ReviewReleaseUploads` / `OpenReviewUploads`, or `release_review_uploads` in `editormcp`) opens the **Review uploads** confirmation dialog listing every artifact (name, path, size), its destination (`itch.io`, `Google Play · internal testing`, `TestFlight`), exact channel/track, version, and keychain credential status before anything is sent. For itch.io targets, channel labels come from `nsgo.yaml`'s `itch.channels` or NSGo's per-target defaults (`html5`, `osx-<arch>`, `windows-<arch>`, `linux-<arch>`). Desktop builds made with the `steam` channel are left out of itch.io and listed as `"upload with SteamPipe"` until a Steam destination exists. A strict trust boundary ensures no upload can ever start without the person confirming in **Review uploads** (`ConfirmReleaseUploads` with a single-use session ID; `StartReleaseUpload` and agent-actor confirmation calls via `WithAgentActor` / `editormcp` are always refused, even under `PermissionProfileAutonomous`). Upload credentials are stored under `PublishKeychainService` through `Editor.Keychain`, a `keychain.Store` from `github.com/nsgo-dev/game/pkg/editor/keychain` (`StorePublishCredential`, `HasPublishCredential`, `DeletePublishCredential` — backed by macOS Keychain, Linux Secret Service, Windows Credential Manager, or `keychain.NewMemory` in tests), handed to the uploader as a redacted `PublishCredential`, and are never written into project files or serialized into snapshots. By default (when `Editor.ReleaseUploader` is nil), confirmed uploads run `go tool nsgo publish` in the project directory (`publish itch -target …` with `BUTLER_API_KEY`, `publish -target android -track internal` with `NSGO_PLAY_SERVICE_ACCOUNT`, and `publish ios <file.ipa>` with `NSGO_APP_STORE_KEY` plus optional `NSGO_APP_STORE_KEY_ID` / `NSGO_APP_STORE_ISSUER` parsed from JSON `{key, key_id, issuer}` or `key_id:issuer` account metadata), passing credentials only via environment variables, streaming output as progress, redacting secrets from logs and errors, and surfacing printed store URLs or processing states.

### Try a build on a phone (LAN QR) or connected device (#1049, #1506)

When a Web or Android build completes on the Build and Release screen, authors can test it directly on their computer, on a phone over local Wi-Fi, or on a USB-connected Android device:

- **LAN-only HTTP server and pure-Go QR code**: finishing a non-dry-run Web build automatically starts a local-network HTTP server (`StartWebBuildServer` / `StopWebBuildServer` / `WebBuildServerState`) bound strictly to a private RFC 1918 LAN IPv4 address (or loopback when offline, customizable via `Editor.LANAddressResolver` and `Editor.WebBuildServePort`). Public bind addresses are refused (`ErrNonLANAddress`), non-LAN peer requests receive `403 Forbidden`, symlinks escaping the build directory are blocked, and stopping the server (via **Stop serving**, closing the editor `Close()`, switching projects, or unmounting `EditorView`) immediately closes the listener and frees the TCP port. The Web result card renders a deterministic scannable ISO/IEC 18004 QR code (`pkg/editor/internal/qrcode`, `EncodeBuildQR`) on a high-contrast light quiet-zone card using Spec tokens alongside the LAN URL, **Copy URL** (`CopyWebBuildURL`), and **Stop serving** / **Start serving** controls.
- **Play-mode MCP bridge over LAN**: served web builds retain exact `index.html` and asset bytes while advertising bridge discovery via `X-NSGo-MCP-Bridge: /__nsgo_mcp/info` and `X-NSGo-Play-Mode: debug` headers. In `js/wasm` web builds, attaching `game/mcp` (`mcp.Attach`) or running `game.Loop.Run` registers `window.__nsgoMCP.callTool` (and `listTools`) backed by the game's live MCP tools (`game_state_dump`, `game_scenes_list`, `game_entities_list`, `game_entity_inspect`, `game_loop_*`, `game_input_*`, `game_console_*`, `game_screenshot`, etc.) and probes the page with `HEAD`: when `X-NSGo-Play-Mode: debug` is present, it fetches `/__nsgo_mcp/info` and loads `/__nsgo_mcp/bridge.js`, authenticates at `POST /__nsgo_mcp/session`, and long-polls `/__nsgo_mcp/poll` + `/__nsgo_mcp/reply` (disconnecting on `pagehide`). From the editor's MCP server (`pkg/editor/editormcp`), agents list connected phone sessions with `phone_sessions_list` and forward calls to them with `phone_call_tool` (governed by `permissions.yaml` `run` rules under `playtests: phone/<tool>` and privacy rules, decoding `game_screenshot` PNGs directly as MCP images), backed by `Editor.WebBridgeSessions`, `Editor.CallWebBridgeTool`, and `Editor.WebBridgeHandler`.
- **RUN ON DEVICE**: shows live device targets and states (`BuildDeviceState`) in the finished build rail:
  - **This Mac's / computer's browser** (`LaunchBuildOnDevice("browser")`, injectable `Editor.OpenBrowserURL`): opens the active LAN URL in the local browser (`ready` -> `running`).
  - **Phone · Wi-Fi** (`LaunchBuildOnDevice("phone-wifi")`): tracks `serving`, `connected` (showing the connected phone's name when attached to the MCP bridge), and `stopped` states.
  - **Connected Android device on USB** (`RefreshConnectedDevices`, `RunBuildOnAndroidDevice`, `WaitAndroidDeviceRun`, `StopAndroidDeviceRun`, injectable `Editor.DeviceDiscoverer` and `Editor.DeviceCommandRunner`): once the Android build finishes, installs and launches via `go -C <dir> tool nsgo build -target <target> -run -device <serial>`, reporting `ready`, `installing`, `running`, `failed`, and `disconnected` states cleanly.
## Project search and Find References (#914)

While a project is open, the file browser's **Search** button (`SearchOpen` / `SetSearchOpen`) toggles the Search & References panel (`SearchPanelView`) below the viewport to jump anywhere in the project or answer "what uses this file?":

- **Project-wide search (`Search`, `SetSearchQuery`, `SearchResults`)**: indexes every project file (following the same walk rules as `ProjectFiles` and `refs.Scan`) and parses every scene file (`*.scene.yaml`, `*.scene.yml`, autoloaded scenes, and scenes referenced by other files). Case-insensitive queries (including space-separated multi-term queries) match file names and paths (`SearchFile`), file UIDs from file headers or `game.index.yaml` (`SearchUID`), scene titles, and inside scenes: object IDs and display names (`SearchObject`, including nested children), component types (`SearchComponent`, including overrides), prefab names (`SearchPrefab`), and instanced or inherited scene paths (`SearchSceneRef`).
- **Jump-to-result (`OpenSearchResult`)**: activating a result for a non-scene file selects it in the file browser (`SelectedFile`). Activating a result for a scene file opens that scene (setting `ScenePath` and `SelectedFile`) and selects the matching object (`SelectedID`), nested children included, falling back to its top-level ancestor when the child cannot be selected. If the scene is already open, it selects the object in place without reloading from disk or discarding unsaved edits and undo history.
- **Find References (`FindReferences`, `ShowReferences`, `ShowSelectedFileReferences`, `References`, `OpenReference`)**: selecting any file or directory in the file browser and pressing **Find Refs** queries `refs.Scan` for every incoming reference, listing each referring file, 1-based line, field, and written value in the exact order `refs.Scan` reports them. Pressing a reference row opens the referring scene (and selects the top-level object owning that line when applicable) or selects a non-scene referring file in the file browser.
- **Live index updates (`RefreshSearch`)**: edits to the currently open scene (`PlaceObject`, `SetObjectName`, `DeleteObject`, Undo/Redo) immediately update active search results in memory; saving scenes, projects, clips, data tables, or localizations, installing/removing packs, and moving files (`MoveFile`, including Undo/Redo, which also follows `ReferencesTarget`) automatically invalidate and refresh the index; external edits on disk are detected via a lightweight directory fingerprint or refreshed explicitly via **Refresh** (`RefreshSearch`). Asynchronous callers can also toggle the panel's loading card with `SearchLoading` / `SetSearchLoading`.
## Running the game and Output panel (#910)

Alongside the existing in-editor **Play** preview (`Play`, `PlayIn`, `Stop`, toggled with `mod+p` / **F5**), the editor can launch the open project itself on desktop via `go tool game dev` and stream its logs into a docked Output panel (`OutputPanelView`) below the viewport:

- **Run and Stop**: choose **Project > Run Game**, `mod+r`, or the Command Palette (`CmdRunGame`) to launch `go tool game dev` in the open project directory (`RunGame` / `RunGameWith`). Enable **Run From Open Scene** (`CmdRunOpenScene`, `SetRunStartAtOpenScene`)—or pass `RunGameOptions{StartAtOpenScene: true}`—to pass `-scene <path>` and `NSGO_GAME_SCENE` (`hotreload.SceneEnv`) for the currently open scene. While the game is running, **Stop Game** (`CmdStopGame`, **Shift+F5** / `StopGame`) terminates the child process and its entire process tree cleanly (via its own process group on Unix and `taskkill /T /F` on Windows). Unmounting `EditorView`, switching projects (`OpenProject`), or calling `Close()` also stops any running game cleanly. Custom tools and tests can inject `Editor.RunGameCommand`.
- **Output / Log panel**: docked below the viewport (`OutputPanelView`, toggleable via the status bar's **Output** button, **View > Panels > Output**, Command Palette `CmdOutputPanel`, or `SetOutputPanelOpen`), streaming the running game's standard output (`OutputStdout`) and standard error (`OutputStderr`) live off the UI goroutine alongside a timestamped history of the editor's own status and error messages (`OutputEditor`). Consecutive editor errors stay in the log instead of overwriting one another.
- **Filter, Clear, and `file:line` navigation**: toggle **Errors** (`SetOutputErrorsOnly`) to show only error entries, press **Clear** (`ClearOutput`) to empty the log, and click any log line carrying a `file:line` location (`OpenOutputEntry`) to open that file in the editor when it is a scene (`*.scene.yaml`, `*.scene.yml`, or listed under `autoload.scenes`) or select it in the file browser otherwise.
## Keyboard shortcuts, menu bar, and command palette (#920)

Every common editor action lives in a single command registry (`Editor.Commands`, `Editor.RegisterCommand`, `Editor.ExecuteCommand`) that drives keyboard shortcuts, the desktop application menu bar, and the Command Palette from one source of truth:

- **Platform shortcuts and text-field guard**: shortcuts use NSGo's `"mod"` modifier (Command on macOS, Ctrl on Windows and Linux; formatted via `FormatShortcut`) and are bound with `core.KeyNotEditing` and `core.MenuNotEditing` so typing or pressing Delete/Backspace/Undo/Save while any text field has focus (such as the inspector's Name field or a search box) never triggers editor shortcuts. When two enabled commands in different scopes share a key, the more specific active scope wins (`shortcutScopePriority`): in 2D with no tile tool active (such as Select), **Viewport** outranks **Tile**, so `e` selects **Rotate** and `r` selects **Scale**; once any tile tool (`b`, `g`, `t`, `u`, `l`, `c`, `n`) is active, **Tile** takes precedence and `e` switches to **Erase Tile**. In 3D (`alt+3`), **3D Viewport** takes `w`, `e`, `r`, `t`, and the axis/camera keys (`1`, `ctrl+1`, `3`, `ctrl+3`, `7`, `ctrl+7`, `5`, `0`); the Lit, Unlit, and Wireframe shading commands under `View > 3D Shading` and `View > Scene Environment` have no shortcut, and `f` (`Frame Selection`) frames the independent 2D `View2D` via `FrameSelection2D` without mutating `Doc.Camera` (and `Camera3D` in 3D). While the right-rail **Agent** tab is active, **Agent** takes `mod+n` (`CmdNewAgentThread`) over `File > New Project...`; while Play mode is running, **Play** takes `mod+l` (`CmdAskFrozenFrame`) over `Ask the Agent About Selection`.
  <!-- BEGIN GENERATED EDITOR SHORTCUTS (EDITOR_UPDATE_SHORTCUT_DOCS=1 go tool nsgo test ./pkg/editor -run TestShortcutDocsMatchRegistry) -->
  - **Global**: `mod+n` (New Project...), `mod+o` (Open Project...), `mod+s` (Save), `mod+w` (Close Scene Tab), `mod+z` (Undo), `mod+shift+z` (Redo), `mod+c` (Copy), `mod+x` (Cut), `mod+v` (Paste), `mod+shift+v` (Paste as Child), `alt+2` (Switch to 2D View), `alt+3` (Switch to 3D View), `mod+l` (Ask the Agent About Selection), `mod+1` (Agent Panel), `mod+2` (Inspector Panel), `mod+k` (Command Palette), `mod+/` (Keyboard Shortcuts), `mod+shift+t` (Background Tasks), `mod+y` (History), `mod+shift+,` (Project Settings), `mod+shift+f` (Project Search & References), `mod+shift+u` (HUD & Menu Layout), `mod+shift+e` (Export & Release), `mod+shift+r` (Build & Release Screen), `mod+shift+m` (Profiler), `mod+shift+b` (Check Problems), `shift+f12` (Find Usages), `mod+,` (Editor Settings), `mod+shift+y` (Playtest Report), `mod+enter` (Keep Agent Turn), `mod+backspace` (Revert Agent Turn), `mod+.` (Stop Agent).
  - **Viewport**: `delete` / `backspace` (Delete Selected Object), `mod+d` (Duplicate Selected Object), `mod+g` (Group Selected Objects), `mod+shift+g` (Ungroup Selected Object), `v` (Tool: Select), `w` / `m` (Tool: Move), `e` (Tool: Rotate), `r` (Tool: Scale), `space` (Tool: Pan), `p` (Tool: Place), `f` (Frame Selection).
  - **2D Viewport**: `d` (Sketch on Viewport), `mod+enter` (Sketch: Go Ahead), `shift+m` (Sketch: Correct a Mark), `o` (Review: Toggle Sketch Overlay), `mod+shift+enter` (Review: Keep Changes and Clear Sketch), `mod+backspace` (Review: Revert Changes and Keep Sketch).
  - **3D Viewport**: `w` (3D Gizmo: Translate), `e` (3D Gizmo: Rotate), `r` (3D Gizmo: Scale), `shift+c` (3D Gizmo: Collider), `1` (3D View: Front), `ctrl+1` (3D View: Back), `3` (3D View: Right), `ctrl+3` (3D View: Left), `7` (3D View: Top), `ctrl+7` (3D View: Bottom), `5` (3D View: Orthographic), `t` (3D Gizmo: Local / Global Space), `0` (3D View: Scene Camera).
  - **Tile**: `b` (Tool: Paint Tile), `e` (Tool: Erase Tile), `g` (Tool: Fill Tile), `t` (Tool: Terrain), `u` (Tool: Rectangle Tile), `l` (Tool: Line Tile), `c` (Tool: Stamp Tile), `n` (Tool: Random Tile).
  - **Sketch**: `p` (Sketch Tool: Pen), `a` (Sketch Tool: Arrow), `b` (Sketch Tool: Box), `t` (Sketch Tool: Text), `i` (Sketch: Add Mark at Cursor), `mod+z` (Sketch: Undo Last Mark), `h` (Sketch: Hide / Show Marks), `mod+shift+backspace` (Sketch: Clear Marks), `shift+d` (Sketch: Finish Sketch Mode), `mod+enter` (Sketch: Send to Agent).
  - **Review**: `j` (Review: Next Change), `k` (Review: Previous Change), `c` (Review: Note for the Agent), `x` (Review: Revert or Restore), `b` (Review: Peek Before Request), `mod+enter` (Review: Keep Changes), `mod+backspace` / `mod+delete` (Review: Revert All).
  - **Play**: `mod+p` / `f5` (Play / Stop), `alt+f` (Mark Play Moment), `mod+l` (Something Looks Wrong), `mod+r` (Run Game).
  - **Agent**: `mod+n` (New Agent Thread).
  <!-- END GENERATED EDITOR SHORTCUTS -->
- **Desktop menu bar**: `EditorView` registers the native application menu bar (`File`, `Edit`, `View`, `Project`, `Help`) through `desktop.SetApplicationMenu` (`Editor.ApplicationMenu`), exposing the same registered commands with live enabled/checked states, formatted shortcut hints, active plugin tools under `View > Tools`, active plugin commands under `Project > Plugins`, and `Help` (`Replay tips`, `Keyboard Shortcuts`).
- **Command Palette (`ui.CommandDialog`)**: press `mod+k` (or the header bar's **Search `⌘K`** button / `View > Command Palette...`) to open `CommandPaletteView`, which lists every built-in and custom command, active plugin commands, active plugin tools (activating them via `SetPluginTool`), active plugin panels (focusing them via `FocusPluginPanel`), and the open project's scenes and files (opening scenes or selecting files via `OpenProjectFile`) with ranked substring and fuzzy matching.
- **Plugin commands**: plugins can declare `Commands []PluginCommand` with an optional `Shortcut`. `RegisterPlugin` normalizes each shortcut (`NormalizeShortcut`) and refuses any shortcut that clashes with a built-in, custom, or other registered plugin command across macOS and Windows/Linux before mutating the editor.
## Asset import settings and rebuilding (#915)

Selecting an image (`.png`, `.jpg`, `.jpeg`, `.webp`), sound (`.wav`, `.ogg`, `.mp3`, `.flac`), font (`.ttf`, `.otf`), or 3D model (`.gltf`, `.glb`) in the file browser (`SelectedFile`, excluding generated target outputs and `*.import.yaml` sidecars) displays its import settings and credit metadata in the Inspector (`InspectorView`):

- **Sidecar and target defaults**: `InspectAssetImport` reads the asset's `*.import.yaml` sidecar when present and overlays it on the matching target's defaults in `game.yaml`'s `assets` section (image `filter`, `format`, `mipmaps`, `srgb`, sprite-sheet `slice`, and frame `pivot`; audio `format`, `rate`, `channels`, `bitrate`, `peakDB`/`rmsDB` normalization, and `loop`; font `size`, `padding`, and `chars`; model `scale`, `animations`, `lodHysteresis`, and `lods`), plus the shared `credit` section (`author`, `license` as an SPDX expression, `source`, and `notice`).
- **Interactive sprite-sheet slice grid and frame pivots (#880)**: when an image is selected, the import panel shows an interactive preview canvas (`DrawImageImportPreview`) over a transparency checkerboard with the live slice grid (`width`, `height`, `margin`, `spacing`, `count`) and draggable pivot markers per cell (or a single marker when unsliced). Authors can drag any marker directly on the preview (`BeginImagePivotDrag` / `UpdateImagePivotDrag` / `EndImagePivotDrag` / `CancelImagePivotDrag`), pick default presets (`TL`, `Center`, `Bottom`), or edit/reset `pivot.default` and per-cell `pivot.cells` numerically (`SelectImportSliceCell`, `SetImageImportPivot`, `SetImageImportDefaultPivot`, `SetImageImportCellPivot`). Every drag commits once to the sidecar as a single undoable step, and running **Reimport** or **Build Assets** writes those pivots into the packed TexturePacker JSON so `sprite.LoadAtlas` loads exact `Frame.PivotX` and `Frame.PivotY` values immediately.
- **Interactive glTF/GLB model preview and animation playback (#897)**: when a `.gltf` or `.glb` model is selected in the file browser, the import panel renders the model through an orbit camera (`DrawModelPreview`, `RenderModelPreview`, `OrbitModelPreview`, `PanModelPreview`, `ZoomModelPreview`, `ResetModelPreviewCamera`) reflecting the effective import `scale`, and inspects its contents (`InspectModelImport`): unscaled and scaled bounds, total triangle and vertex counts, and lists of its nodes, meshes, materials, and animations. Each animation can be selected, played, paused, stopped, looped, or scrubbed (`SelectModelPreviewAnimation`, `PlayModelPreviewAnimation`, `PauseModelPreviewAnimation`, `StopModelPreviewAnimation`, `SeekModelPreviewAnimation`, `StepModelPreviewAnimation`), supporting node TRS transforms, skeletal skinning, and morph targets. Editing `scale`, `animations`, `lodHysteresis`, or `credit` writes the deterministic sidecar through `importsettings.Encode` and shows which `game.yaml` model target reads the file, so the next `game assets` run rebuilds only that model.
- **Validated undoable edits and field reset**: every change writes the sidecar atomically through `importsettings.Encode` as one undoable `Editor.History` step (`EditAssetImportSettings`, typed `SetImageImport*` / `SetAudioImport*` / `SetFontImport*` / `SetModelImport*` setters, and `SetAssetImportCredit`). **Undo** restores the exact previous sidecar bytes verbatim (or removes a newly created sidecar file), and **Reset** (`ResetAssetImportField`) clears an override back to the target's default, removing the sidecar once no overrides or credit remain.
- **Reimport, Build Assets, and live reload**: the inspector's **Reimport** button (`ReimportAsset`) and the Command Palette's **Build Assets** command (`CmdBuildAssets`, `BuildAssets` / `BuildAssetsWith`) run `go tool game assets` off the UI goroutine, streaming stdout and stderr into the docked **Output** panel (`OutputPanelView`). On completion, `ReloadAssets` reloads changed atlases and textures from disk into `Editor.Resolver` (updating `sceneformat.AtlasMap` or existing `*sprite.Atlas` values in place and invoking `AssetReloader` / `OnReloadAssets` when set), refreshes the Asset Browser and Viewport immediately without restarting the editor, and re-runs the Problems check. Custom tools and tests can inject `Editor.AssetsCommandRunner`.

## Profiler panel and frame time strip (#912)

While playing in the editor or connected to a running game over MCP (`game_perf_readout`, `game_perf_zones`, `game_perf_reset`), the editor displays a live frame-time strip beneath the viewport and opens a full Profiler panel styled with the editor's design spec tokens (`design/editor/PlayMode.dc.html` and `Spec.dc.html`):

- **Play-mode `FRAME TIME · LAST 60 S` strip (`ProfilerStripView`)**: always visible below the game view while Play mode is active or a remote game is connected (`ConnectProfiler`). It shows `avg`, `p99`, and `allocs/frame` from `game_perf_readout`, threshold lines and legend markers at `8.3 ms` (`over 8.3 ms`) and `16.7 ms` (`missed 16.7 ms`), the `−60 s`, `−30 s`, and `now` axis, and a callout pill labelling spikes with their cause when the game supplies one via `gamedebug.Overlay.NoteFrameCause` (or `"GC pause"` on GC hitches). Pressing the strip's **Profiler** button opens the full Profiler panel.
- **Connecting to a running game**: connect via `ConnectProfilerMCP` (official MCP Go SDK over any `mcp.Transport`), `ConnectProfilerCommand` (spawning `go tool nsgo mcp`), `ConnectProfilerScope` (an in-process `*core.Scope` with `mcp.Bridge` attached), or `NewBridgeProfilerClient`, and pass the client to `Editor.ConnectProfiler`. While attached to a scope, the editor polls `game_perf_readout` and `game_perf_zones` every `Editor.ProfilerPollInterval` (default `250 ms`), or on demand via `Editor.PollProfiler`. Built-in Play mode (`Play` / `PlayIn`) also instruments its loop with `gamedebug.Overlay`, `gamedebug.Profiler`, and `gamedebug.GCMonitor` automatically when no external profiler client is connected.
- **Full Profiler panel (`ProfilerPanelView`)**: toggleable from the strip, status bar **Debugger** button, `Project > Profiler`, Command Palette, or `mod+shift+m` (`CmdProfiler` / `SetProfilerOpen`). It displays live KPI cards and switchable time-series charts (`SetProfilerMetric`) for **Frame** (stacked update, draw, total, and GC hitch markers with 8.3 ms / 16.7 ms lines), **FPS**, **Allocs & GC** (allocations per frame, live heap bytes, and GC hitches), and **Memory** (tracked asset memory against budget and per-group totals), alongside a scrollable table of named timing zones from `game_perf_zones` sorted by cost.
- **Reset and Chrome/Perfetto trace export**: pressing **Reset** (`Editor.ResetProfiler`) invokes `game_perf_reset` on the connected game and clears the editor's recorded history and zone table. Pressing **Export Trace** (or calling `Editor.ExportProfilerTrace` / `Editor.ExportProfilerTraceFile`, or intercepting via `Editor.OnExportProfilerTrace`) writes the recorded frames and zones as Chrome/Perfetto Trace Event Format JSON through `gamedebug.Profiler.WriteChromeTrace`.
## Start screen (#922)

Running `go tool game editor` with no arguments opens the Start Screen (`StartScreenView`, toggled with `SetStartScreenOpen`), designed from `design/editor/Start.dc.html`, step 1 of `FirstRun.dc.html`, and the `Spec.dc.html` tokens:

- **Recent projects (`RecentProjects`, `ParseRecentProjects`, `EncodeRecentProjects`)**: opening a project records its name, directory path, and UTC RFC3339 `lastOpened` timestamp in a strict versioned per-user YAML file outside any project (`<UserConfigDir>/nsgo-game/recent-projects.yaml` via `DefaultRecentProjectsPath`, or `Editor.RecentProjectsPath`). The sidebar's **RECENT** list appears once at least one project has been opened, displays relative times, marks entries whose folder or `game.yaml` is missing on disk (`Missing`) with a badge and remove action (`RemoveRecentProject`), and persists across restarts.
- **Open folder (`BrowseAndOpenProject`)**: opens a project directory through NSGo's native folder picker (`core.Scope.PickDirectories`) or the injectable `Editor.PickProjectFolder` hook.
- **New project from template (`ProjectTemplates`, `StartCreateProject`)**: template cards and `All` / `2D` / `3D` filter chips are populated dynamically from `game new`'s template registry, so newly added templates appear without editor changes. The project name, parent folder (defaulting to `~/games`), and optional module path are validated live as typed (`ValidateNewProjectInputs`): checking `a–z`, `0–9`, and `-` characters, write access to the parent folder, and existing folders (offering **Open it** or the next free sibling name such as `dungeon-run-2`). Pressing **Create** runs `game new` (including its `go get` and `go mod tidy` steps) off the UI goroutine while streaming stdout and stderr into the Start Screen, then opens the created `game.yaml`.
- **Open Demo (`OpenDemo`, `NewDemo`)**: opens the built-in Demo Room scene without creating or opening a project on disk.
- **Start with AI — from one sentence to the first review (#1051, #1505)**: pressing **Start with AI** (`StartWithAI`) runs the five-stage flow in `design/editor/FirstRun.dc.html` backed by the connected agent and the editor's MCP server:
  - **WHAT HAPPENS NEXT & Connect in place**: the Start Screen lists the three steps (**Connect an agent**, **Approve its plan**, **Review the first scene**). When no agent is connected yet, **Start with AI** opens **Connect an agent** (`FirstRunStageConnect`) in place on the Start Screen while preserving the idea and project name (with **Edit** and **Later** returning cleanly to Step 1). It detects `claude` and `codex` on `PATH` (or via `Editor.FirstRunDetectAgents`), connects by querying the editor's MCP server tool count (`Editor.AgentTools`) and running a readiness probe through `Editor.AgentRunner` / `CLIAgentRunner` (failing visibly with diagnostics when the MCP server or CLI cannot answer, replaceable via `Editor.FirstRunConnectAgent`), or validates a pasted API key immediately with a minimal request to `BuiltInAgentEndpoint` (never written to YAML, replaceable via `Editor.FirstRunValidateAPIKey`).
  - **The first plan (`FirstRunStagePlan`, `OpenFirstRunPlan`)**: asks the connected agent to propose a plan tailored to the author's idea via `plan_propose` (or a structured JSON plan response, replaceable via `Editor.FirstRunPlanner`), displaying any clarifying question, numbered steps, and dashed-violet ghosts (`FirstRunGhost`) beside the read-only template preview viewport with **Just do it** (`JustDoItFirstRunPlan`) and **Approve ⌘↵** (`ApproveFirstRunPlan`). Planning runs off the UI goroutine when mounted so MCP `plan_propose` calls never block the UI, and fails visibly with **Try again** when the agent cannot produce a plan. No project folder is created on disk until **Approve**.
  - **Building (`FirstRunStageBuilding`)**: after **Approve** creates the brand-new folder, the editor scaffolds the project, opens the target scene, asks the connected agent to build it via `scene_edit` (streaming live object counts and placed object labels from the scene), runs `project.Check` and `assetcheck`, and runs `go tool game playtest` (or `Editor.FirstRunPlaytest`) when `playtest.yaml` exists — or marks Playtest **Skipped** with an honest label when the template has no `playtest.yaml`. Pressing **Cancel** or `Esc` pauses and asks first (**Keep building**, **Stop, keep the files**, or **Stop and delete the folder** — offered and permitted only for the brand-new folder created by this flow, never touching sibling or pre-existing folders). A failed `Check` stops before the review with a proposed fix (**Fix it** / **Review as is**).
  - **The first review & one-time tips (`FirstRunStageReview`)**: derives its change list, counts, shapes, and deviations directly from the actual project files and scene written on disk, displaying the plan summary on top, **Before / After** toggle plus hold-`B` peek (`Before: an empty folder`), per-change revert/restore toggles, **Revert all**, and **Keep N of M** (`KeepFirstRunReview`). Three onboarding tips (**keep or revert each change**, **hold B**, **select then ⌘L**) show one at a time and once per person, stored under `seenTips` in `EditorSettings` (`editor.yaml`); **Skip tips** ends all three and **`Help › Replay tips`** (`CmdReplayTips` / `ReplayTips`) clears them and brings them back.
- **Clean project switching and unsaved-edits prompt**: opening a project from the Start Screen or later through `File > Open Project...` (`mod+o`), `File > New Project...` (`mod+n` outside the right-rail **Agent** tab, where `ScopeAgent` binds `mod+n` to `CmdNewAgentThread` instead), or `File > Start Screen` replaces the current project, scene, clip, cutscene, data table, localization workspace, skeletal rig, and undo history cleanly. Whenever the open session has unsaved edits (`HasUnsavedChanges`), the editor's unified unsaved-changes dialog asks to **Save** (`ConfirmSaveAndSwitch`), **Discard** (`ConfirmDiscardAndSwitch`), or **Cancel** (`CancelPendingSwitch`) before replacing anything.

## 3D LOD and occluder visualization (#904)

In 3D view (`View3D`), the viewport runs the same `scenemodel.Build` + `render3d.OcclusionBuffer` + `render3d.Queue` evaluation used at runtime (automatically backed by `scenemodel.NewAssets` when a project is open, or an injected `Editor.ModelResolver`):

- **LOD overlay (`ShowLODOverlay` / `SetShowLODOverlay`)**: color-codes each multi-LOD object by its active level and draws a `LOD<n>` badge beside it in the viewport; the 3D inspector also reports the selected object's active LOD (`ObjectLOD3D`, `ObjectLODCount3D`). Editing a model's `lods` or `lodHysteresis` in the asset import inspector invalidates the 3D build so switches take effect immediately.
- **Occluder visualization and editing (`ShowOccluders` / `SetShowOccluders`, `SetObjectOccluder`)**: draws magenta wireframes with `OCC` / `OCC BOX` badges for every visible occluder (including explicit `box` occluders and `model` occluders), dims objects culled behind an occluder (`ObjectOccluded3D`), and exposes undoable Occluder mode (`none` / `model` / `box`), box size, and double-sided controls in the 3D inspector (raising version 1 scenes to version 2 automatically).
- **RenderStats overlay (`ShowRenderStats` / `SetShowRenderStats`, `RenderStats3D`)**: displays the live `render3d.RenderStats` readout (`Draws`, `Visible`, `Occluded`, `Tris`, `Verts`, plus active per-object `LOD` levels) in the bottom-right corner of the 3D viewport, toggleable alongside the LOD and Occluders overlays from the 3D viewport bar's **Overlays** popover (`view3d-overlays`), `View > Overlays`, or the Command Palette (`CmdToggleLODOverlay`, `CmdToggleOccluders`, `CmdToggleRenderStats`).

## Remote scene tree and live inspector (#911)

While a game built with [`game/mcp`](mcp.md) runs with `-automation`, the editor connects as an MCP client (using `github.com/modelcontextprotocol/go-sdk/mcp` over `go tool nsgo mcp` on desktop, or any `mcp.Transport` passed to `ConnectRemote` / `Editor.RemoteTransport`) to inspect and tune the running game live:

- **Connecting**: enable **Auto-connect on Run** in the **Remote** tab (`SetRunWithAutomation`) or call `RunGameWith(RunGameOptions{Automation: true})` to pass `-automation` to `go tool game dev` and connect automatically once the game starts, or call `ConnectRemote` / `ConnectRemoteCommand` to attach to an already-running game. `NewRemoteBridgeServer(bridge, run)` wraps a `*mcp.Bridge` directly as an MCP server for deterministic in-process or pipe testing.
- **Remote tab beside the Scene Tree**: switch between **Scene** (`SceneTreeLocal`) and **Remote** (`SceneTreeRemote`) in `SceneTreeView`. The Remote tab lists active scenes (`game_scenes_list`), layers in draw order (`game_layers_list`), and live entities with their parent-child hierarchy (`game_entities_list`), refreshed automatically while unpaused (interval `Editor.RemoteRefreshInterval`, default 250 ms) or on demand via `RefreshRemote`. Entities without a scene `ObjectRef` group under **SPAWNED AT RUNTIME**, and scene objects that are destroyed while running appear dimmed with the step they were taken at.
- **Live Inspector and Live Changes**: selecting a live entity (`SelectRemoteEntity`) inspects its components (`game_entity_inspect`) in `InspectorView`. Read-only derived components (`world_transform2d`, `object_ref`) show live readouts, while editable components render typed controls that patch the running game immediately through `game_component_set` (`SetRemoteComponentField` / `SetRemoteComponentValue`) and display `"was X"` beside changed fields. Every live edit is recorded in **LIVE CHANGES** (`RemoteLiveChanges`), which stays after disconnecting or stopping the game: revert individual rows (`RevertRemoteLiveChange`), discard all (`DiscardRemoteLiveChanges`), or press **Keep in scene** (`KeepRemoteLiveChangesInScene`) to write all changes on scene-backed entities into the open scene as a single undoable `History.Group` step that one Undo restores.
- **Live Play Bar, Tunables, and Console**: while connected, the Play Bar exposes **Pause / Resume** (`mod+shift+p`, `RemotePause` / `RemoteResume`), **Step** (`.` while paused) and **Step N** fixed updates (`RemoteStep`), **Time Scale** (`0.25×`, `0.5×`, `1×`, `2×`, `RemoteSetTimeScale`), and live frame/time/entity readouts in the status bar. When the game configures a `gamedebug.Console`, the Remote Inspector also lists live **Tunables** (`game_tunables_list` / `RemoteSetTunable`) and a **Console** command line (`RemoteExecConsole` / `game_console_exec`).
## 3D terrain sculpting and painting (#900)

In 3D view (`ViewMode3D`), choose **Project > 3D Terrain Editor** or the Command Palette (`CmdTerrainEditor`)—or open a `*.terrain.yaml` file from the file browser via double-click / **Terrain**, or call `NewTerrain`, `OpenTerrainFile`, `OpenTerrainProjectFile`, or `OpenTerrain` in Go—to edit a `world3d` terrain directly in the 3D viewport with the docked `TerrainPanelView` below it:

- **Sculpt and paint brushes**: choose `TerrainBrushRaise`, `TerrainBrushLower`, `TerrainBrushSmooth`, `TerrainBrushFlatten` (targeting the height sampled where the stroke starts, or `TargetHeight` when `UseTargetHeight` is set), `TerrainBrushSetHeight` (`TargetHeight`), `TerrainBrushPaintLayer` (increasing the active texture layer's weight while keeping all layers normalized to 1), or `TerrainBrushPaintFoliage` (blending the active foliage layer's density toward `TargetDensity` in `[0, 1]`, where `0` erases). Configure `Radius`, `Strength`, and `Falloff` (`TerrainFalloffSmooth`, `TerrainFalloffLinear`, `TerrainFalloffFlat`) via `SetTerrainBrush` or the panel controls.
- **3D raycast strokes and single-step Undo/Redo**: clicking or dragging across the terrain in the 3D viewport raycasts against the live `world3d.Heightmap` (`RaycastTerrain3D`, `BeginTerrainStrokeAt` / `ContinueTerrainStrokeAt` / `EndTerrainStroke`, or world-coordinate `BeginTerrainStroke` / `ContinueTerrainStroke`), interpolates continuous dabs along fast drags, draws a terrain-conforming brush radius ring overlay, and records each complete stroke as **one undo step** in `Editor.History` (with `Escape` / `CancelTerrainStroke` aborting an active stroke cleanly). Undo and Redo restore exact heightmap, layer-weight, and foliage-density samples and track `TerrainDirty()` / `HasUnsavedChanges()`.
- **Lossless 16-bit saving**: **Save Terrain** (`SaveTerrain`, `SaveTerrainTo`, or `Save` when dirty) writes the 16-bit grayscale PNG heightmap (`*image.Gray16`, eliminating 8-bit slope banding), per-layer grayscale weight-map PNGs (or packed RGBA `splatMaps`), foliage density-map PNGs, and deterministic `*.terrain.yaml` atomically so `world3d.LoadTerrainFS`, `TerrainDoc.ScatterFoliage`, and `render3d.TerrainMaterialFromDoc` load and draw the exact same terrain in the game.
## Particle emitter editor (#883)

The Particle Emitter Editor (`ParticleEditorPanelView`), opened via **Project > Particle Emitter Editor**, the Command Palette (`CmdParticleEditor`), by double-clicking a `*.particles.yaml` / `*.emitter.yaml` file or pressing **Open Emitter** in the file browser, or from the Inspector's **Edit Emitter** button on an object with `particles`, edits every field of a 2D `particles.Config` with live preview and full Undo/Redo:

- **Live preview without restart**: the panel runs a live `*particles.Emitter` (`ParticlePreview`) on a preview canvas with **Play/Pause** (`PlayParticlePreview` / `PauseParticlePreview`), **Restart** (`RestartParticlePreview`), **Step** (`StepParticlePreview`), **Burst** (`BurstParticlePreview`), **Preview Atlas** (`SetParticlePreviewAtlas`), and an optional **Floor** collider (`SetParticlePreviewFloor`) to test bounce/stop/kill collisions. Editing rate, curves, motion, or other fields calls `Emitter.ReloadConfig` so the running preview reflects changes live without restarting or clearing live particles. When no atlas or frames are bound, the preview draws colored fallback discs so particles remain visible while tuning.
- **Every emitter field & interactive curve widget**: edits `space`, position, rotation, `z`, `seed`, `shape` (`point`, `circle`, `ring`, `rect`), `rate`, `maxParticles`, scheduled `bursts` (add/edit/remove `time`, `count`, `cycles`, `interval`), `lifetimeMin`/`Max`, `speedMin`/`Max`, `angleMin`/`Max`, `gravityX`/`Y`, `initialRotationMin`/`Max`, `rotationSpeedMin`/`Max`, `collision`, `trail`, and recursive `subEmitters` (navigate into any child config via `SetParticleTargetPath` / **Edit Child**). The curve widget plots `sizeCurve`, `alphaCurve`, `rotationCurve`, `colorCurve`, `trail.widthCurve`, and `trail.colorGradient` on an interactive canvas where stops can be dragged, clicked to add, or edited numerically, and the atlas frame picker toggles `frameNames` directly from the resolver's atlases.
- **Undo/Redo and deterministic save**: every edit goes through `Editor.History`, and **Save Emitter** (`SaveParticleEmitterFile` / `SaveParticleEmitterYAML` / `OnSaveParticleEmitter`) writes canonical versioned YAML (`particles.MarshalConfigYAML`) that round-trips unchanged and hot-reloads matching emitters already placed in the open scene.
- **Scene placement**: bind any object's `particles` field via `SetObjectParticles`, `PlaceParticles`, or the Inspector's **PARTICLES** section (`file` and `atlas` pickers). Placed emitters animate live in the 2D edit viewport (`StepSceneEmitters`, hidden when the object is hidden), run in Play mode (`PlayIn`) and in the game (`sceneformat.Build`), are scanned by `tooling/refs` (`MoveFile` rewrites `particles.file`), and are validated by `assetcheck`.
## Language and appearance settings (#921)

The editor UI is localized in English (`en`) and Simplified Chinese (`zh`) through embedded versioned `l10n` YAML string tables (`editor/locales/en.yaml`, `editor/locales/zh.yaml`), and per-user preferences are stored outside any project as deterministic versioned YAML:

- **Per-user settings file (`EditorSettings`, `SettingsVersion = 1`, #921, #1005, #1006, #1028)**: stored at `DefaultSettingsPath()` (under `os.UserConfigDir()` as `nsgo-game/editor.yaml`, outside every game project) and loaded/saved with `ParseEditorSettings`, `EncodeEditorSettings`, `LoadEditorSettings`, `SaveEditorSettings`, `Editor.LoadSettings`, `Editor.LoadUserSettings`, and `Editor.SaveSettings`. It stores `version`, `language`, `appearance`, optional `autoSave` (defaults to `true` when omitted), optional `interfaceSize` (`compact`, `default`, or `large`), optional `snap` (per-person defaults for `grid`, `gridSize`, `pixel`, `rotation`, `rotationStepDeg`, `scale`, `scaleStep`, and `showGrid`, used when a project does not override snap in `.game/editor.yaml`), optional `showAgentChanges` (defaults to `true` when omitted), optional `navigation3D` (`default`, `blender`, or `maya`), optional `viewportOverlays` (canonical sorted list of enabled 2D viewport overlay IDs; omitted for defaults, `[]` for all off), optional `overlayLegend` (canonical sorted list of enabled Spec viewport overlay legend kinds: `selection`, `agent`, `added`, `changed`, `removed`, `problem`; omitted enables all six, `[]` hides all six), optional `shortcuts`, optional `seenTips`, and optional `agent` preferences (task routing, MCP tool toggles, external MCP servers, weekly request limit, outbound privacy switches, and local usage counters; secrets such as API keys are never stored in `editor.yaml`). Unknown keys, YAML anchors/aliases/merge keys, trailing documents, negative/non-finite snap steps, and invalid enum values are refused with `*loaderr.Error`. Output is deterministic 2-space YAML prefixed with `SettingsSchemaComment` so a load-save round trip changes nothing byte-for-byte. Its published JSON Schema is `https://game.nsgo.dev/schemas/editor-settings.v1.json` (`SettingsSchemaURL`).
  ```yaml
  # yaml-language-server: $schema=https://game.nsgo.dev/schemas/editor-settings.v1.json
  version: 1
  language: system
  appearance: system
  autoSave: true
  interfaceSize: default
  snap:
    grid: true
    gridSize: 16
    rotation: true
    rotationStepDeg: 15
    showGrid: true
  showAgentChanges: true
  navigation3D: default
  shortcuts:
    view.frame: g
  ```
- **Unified Settings window (`SettingsView`, `⌘,` / `Esc`, #1005)**:
  - **Opening, closing, and focus restoration**: pressing `⌘,` (`mod+,`, `CmdEditorSettings`) opens the full-window Settings view (with or without an open project) and pressing `Esc` closes it, returning keyboard focus to the exact control that had focus before opening.
  - **Grouped navigation and page anatomy**: the left navigation list is grouped into **`AGENT`** (Models and connections, Permissions, Game brief, Project rules, Tools, Usage and privacy), **`PROJECT · game.yaml`** (Project, Input map, Audio buses, Export — hidden when no project is open), and **`EDITOR · THIS COMPUTER`** (General, Viewport, Shortcuts), with a live meta line on each entry and no empty group headings. Each page shows its title, one-line subtitle, and scope chip (`"This computer"`, `"Project · shared"`, or `"Project · game.yaml"`), and each row renders a label, help text, value control, and `"Agent may change"` or `"You only"` policy column.
  - **Search and agent handoff**: typing into the top search field (`"Search settings, or tell the agent what you want"`) matches setting labels and help text across all pages, narrowing both the sidebar navigation (hiding non-matching pages and empty groups) and the rows on the active page. When no setting matches and the agent panel is enabled, Settings offers sending the query directly to the agent (`AskAgentFromSettings`).
  - **General page (`SettingsPageGeneral`)**: edits **Language** (`Editor.SetLanguage`), **Appearance** (`Editor.SetAppearance`), **Save automatically** (`Editor.SetAutoSave`, kept changes only after 2 s), and **Interface size** (`Editor.InterfaceSize`, `Editor.SetInterfaceSize`: `Compact`, `Default`, `Large`, scaling text and controls together).
